	+ Desire and purpose can lead to a great clamor and yearning for something we don't have
	+ This can undermine the peace and quiet of the soul
	+ However, this is not a problem if we have abandoned ourselves to God in faith and love

• **Abandoning Self to God**
	+ When we abandon ourselves to God, we must let go of our own self and all attachments
	+ We must transfer our being and powers to God, and let Him act within us
	+ This is necessary for spiritual growth and union with God

• **The Nature of the Soul**
	+ The soul is like a vessel that receives God's Word and passes it on to our powers
	+ The Word is God's, and not our own
	+ We must accept all that happens within us as God's work, and not our own

• **The Role of the Holy Spirit**
	+ The Holy Spirit prays within us, and not we ourselves
	+ We must let go of our own efforts and let the Holy Spirit work within us
	+ This is necessary for spiritual growth and union with God

• **The Importance of Laying Claim to Nothing**
	+ We must lay claim to nothing, and let God act within us as He will
	+ This is necessary for spiritual growth and union with God
	+ We must become like a desert, empty and receptive to God's work

• **The Role of Human Effort**
	+ We should not wait passively for God to work, but rather engage in good occupations like prayer and scripture study
	+ However, we must not rely on our own efforts, but rather let God work within us
	+ Our efforts should be directed towards letting go of ourselves and making room for God's work

• **The Goal of Spiritual Growth**
	+ The goal of spiritual growth is to become like a desert, empty and receptive to God's work
	+ This is necessary for union with God and spiritual maturity
	+ We must let go of our own self and all attachments, and let God act within us as He will.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **The purpose of outward works**
	+ To direct the outer man to God and train him for spiritual living
	+ To prevent straying into ineptitudes and worldly desires
	+ To prepare the outer man for God's work in his soul
* **The role of virtues and disciplines**
	+ To restrain the outer man from worldly and ungodly things
	+ To help the inner man stay close to God
	+ To make it easier for God to work in the soul
* **The importance of inwardness**
	+ If a man is well-trained in true inwardness, he can drop outward disciplines
	+ Inwardness is more important than outward practices
	+ True inwardness releases a man from his vows and contracts with God
* **Vows and contracts with God**
	+ Vows are contracts with God that cannot be released by humans
	+ Entering into true inwardness releases a man from his vows
	+ God will answer for the time spent in inwardness
* **Performing vows after returning to outward practices**
	+ A man should perform his vows for the present time
	+ He does not need to make up for time spent in inwardness
	+ God will make up for the time spent in inwardness
* **The value of outward practices**
	+ Any work that brings a man closer to God is the best
	+ Outward practices can be useful for the outer man, but not necessary for the inner man
* **The difference between vows taken before a priest and vows taken to God**
	+ Vows taken before a priest are binding, but not necessarily the best
	+ Vows taken to God Himself are more important and can be released if a better way is found
* **The importance of inward truth**
	+ Inward truth is more important than outward practices
	+ The letter (outward practices) kills, but the spirit (inward truth) gives life
* **Following the spirit rather than the letter**
	+ A man should follow the inward truth and spirit rather than outward practices
	+ He should take good note of this and follow it above all else
* **The value of simplicity and experience**
	+ A simple layman can release himself from vows if he finds them hampering
	+ Experience and simplicity are more important than outward practices

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **Sermon Three**
	+ The speaker emphasizes the importance of a quiet and detached spirit when praying.
	+ The goal is to seek inner peace and silence, allowing the eternal Word to be spoken within.
	+ The speaker quotes Matthew 6:7, warning against using many words in prayer.
* **The Birth of the Word**
	+ The sermon is part of a treatise on the Birth of the Word.
	+ The historical birth of Jesus is distinct from the "eternal birth".
	+ The speaker notes that the stress on force (gewalt) is unusual for Eckhart.
* **The Role of the Intellect and Will**
	+ As a Dominican, Eckhart places the intellect above the will.
	+ This is in contrast to the Franciscans, who prioritize the will.
	+ Eckhart had debated this question with the Franciscan General Gonsalvus.
* **The Importance of Inner Silence**
	+ The speaker emphasizes the need to leave behind the "crowd" of worldly thoughts and desires.
	+ This includes memory, understanding, and will, as well as sense perceptions and imagination.
	+ The goal is to find inner peace and silence, allowing the eternal Word to be spoken within.
* **The Limitations of Human Knowledge**
	+ The speaker notes that human knowledge and reason are not sufficient to understand God.
	+ Instead, one must surrender to God's knowledge and will.
	+ The natural light of human understanding must become "pure nothing" to allow God to shine through.
* **The Story of Jesus in the Temple**
	+ The speaker uses the story of Jesus in the Temple (Luke 2:42) to illustrate the need to leave behind worldly distractions.
	+ Jesus is found in the Temple, but only after his parents have returned to the source and ground whence they came.
	+ The speaker notes that Jesus was never found among friends or acquaintances, but only in the Temple.
* **The Need to Surrender to God**
	+ The speaker emphasizes the need to surrender to God's will and knowledge.
	+ This involves ceasing all activity and allowing God to work through one's powers.
	+ The goal is to allow God to shine through, rather than relying on human understanding or reason.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Limitations of Human Knowledge**
	+ Human knowledge and wisdom cannot enable us to know God divinely.
	+ Our knowing must become a pure unknowing, and a forgetting of ourselves and all creatures.

• **The Role of Intellect**
	+ Our intellect is supposed to be empty and functionless in order to know God.
	+ This is not a state of ignorance, but rather a potential receptivity.

• **The Nature of Darkness**
	+ The darkness referred to is not a lack of being, but rather a potential receptivity.
	+ It is a state of being perfected and filled with all being.

• **The Consequences of Turning Back**
	+ If we turn back from this state of darkness, it is not due to any truth, but rather due to external influences.
	+ This can lead to sin and eternal backsliding.

• **The Importance of Pressing Forward**
	+ There is no turning back, but only a pressing forward to attain and achieve this possibility.
	+ The mind of man has the potential to outstrip the highest heaven if it remains undefiled and unrent.

• **The Profits of Pursuing this Possibility**
	+ Pursuing this possibility contains the chance to gain Him who is all things.
	+ The more barren we are of self and unwitting of all things, the nearer we are to Him.

• **The Role of Solitude**
	+ The true word of eternity is spoken only in solitude, where a man is a desert and alien to himself and multiplicity.
	+ This desolate self-estrangement is where we find peace and rest.

• **The Importance of Rejection and Estrangement**
	+ Rejection, desolation, and estrangement from all creatures is where we find peace and rest.
	+ David says he would rather be rejected and spurned in the house of God than dwell with great honor and wealth in the tavern of sinners.

• **The Necessity of Being Barren**
	+ It is not always necessary to be barren and estranged from everything, but it is a necessary step in pursuing this possibility.
	+ This is a grievous matter for God to leave a man without support.

• **The Goal of Human Aspiration**
	+ The goal of human aspiration is to attain and achieve this possibility of being filled with all being.
	+ This is a state of being perfected and filled with all being, where we are fed and cherished by the Greatest Good.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **God's Action is Not Delayed**
	+ God's action is not delayed, but rather, it is instantaneous when the person is ready.
	+ God does not work at His own pace, but rather, He acts as soon as the person is prepared.

• **Preparation is Not in Our Hands**
	+ We cannot prepare ourselves for God's action, as it is impossible to do so.
	+ God must act and pour Himself into us the moment He finds us ready.

• **God's Nature is Not Like a Human's**
	+ God's nature is not like a human carpenter's, who works or not as He likes.
	+ God must act when He finds us ready, just as the sun bursts forth when the air is clear and pure.

• **The Instant of Readiness is the Moment of Action**
	+ When nature reaches its highest point, God gives grace.
	+ The instant the spirit is ready, God enters without hesitation or delay.

• **God Stands at the Door of Our Heart**
	+ God is no further than the door of our heart, waiting for us to open up and let Him in.
	+ He longs for us a thousand times more than we long for Him.

• **Awareness of God is Not in Our Power**
	+ Our being aware of God is not in our power, but in His.
	+ When it suits Him, He shows Himself, and He can hide when He wishes.

• **God Fills the Void**
	+ God cannot leave anything void or unfilled.
	+ Even if we think we can't feel Him, that is not the case, as God must fill the void.

• **Three Certain Signs of God's Action**
	+ There are three certain signs of God's action, but only one is mentioned.
	+ The first sign is not mentioned, but the second and third signs are discussed.

• **The Sign of Timelessness**
	+ A man can reach the point where he is no longer hindered by time.
	+ This is one of the signs of God's action, but it is not the only one.

• **The Importance of Emptiness**
	+ Emptiness is essential for God's action to take place.
	+ We must stand still and not waver from our emptiness, as this is the moment of birth.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Birth of Spiritual Awareness**
	+ Occurs when one is fully turned towards God
	+ No external hindrances can prevent this birth
	+ All things become a means to experience God

• **The Analogy of Lightning**
	+ Strikes everything, turning them towards itself
	+ Similarly, when one experiences the birth of spiritual awareness, they are turned towards God

• **The Role of Penitential Exercises**
	+ Were instituted to curb the flesh and help the spirit
	+ However, love is a more effective means of overcoming the flesh

• **The Power of Love**
	+ Love is a sweet burden that brings one closer to God
	+ It is a stronger bond than any penitential exercise

• **The Benefits of Love**
	+ Allows one to cheerfully bear and endure hardships
	+ Enables one to forgive evil and make progress in their spiritual journey

• **The Importance of Love**
	+ Is written as being as strong as death and as hard as hell (Song 8:6)
	+ Separates all things from the soul, making them intolerant of anything that is not God

• **The Fruitfulness of Love**
	+ Makes even the smallest actions or practices profitable and fruitful
	+ Is more pleasing to God than all the works of others

• **The Sufficiency of Love**
	+ A man who has found the way of love needs no other means
	+ He is caught in the net of love and all his actions are pleasing to God

• **The Overcoming of the Flesh**
	+ Love is a more effective means of overcoming the flesh than penitential exercises
	+ It loads the flesh with a pleasant burden, making it easier to resist

• **The Bond of Love**
	+ Is a strong and pleasant bond that makes one's entire being belong to God
	+ It is the key to experiencing the birth of spiritual awareness and living a life pleasing to God

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **God is love**
	+ God is love because He pursues all that can love Him with His love
	+ God is love because all He created pursues Him with love
	+ God is love because He drives all plurality out of existence with His love
	+ God is love because He gives all creatures their being and life with His love

• **God's love is essential to His nature**
	+ God's love is as essential to His nature as His truth and goodness
	+ To stop God loving something would be to deprive Him of His Godhead

• **The nature of divine love**
	+ Divine love is not taken into us, but rather takes us into itself
	+ We are one with divine love, just as the paint on a wall is maintained by the wall

• **The relationship between God and creatures**
	+ All creatures are maintained in existence by love, which is God
	+ If deprived of love, all things would lose their existence

• **The difference between spiritual and bodily things**
	+ Spiritual things can dwell in another, but bodily things cannot
	+ Every spiritual thing can exist in another, but not bodily things

• **The interconnectedness of all things**
	+ Every angel is in the next with all their joy and happiness
	+ Every angel is in me, and so is God Himself with all His beatitude

• **The power of even the smallest part of a spiritual being**
	+ The smallest splinter or spark from an angel can light up the whole world with bliss and joy

• **The splendor of spiritual beings**
	+ Angels are beyond all number and are splendid in themselves

• **The importance of loving God**
	+ We should love God with all our heart and soul
	+ God is so loveable that all creatures seek to love His loveableness, whether they know it or not

• **The nature of evil**
	+ Whatever one loves must either seem good or be good
	+ There is no creature so worthless that it could love anything evil

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Nature of Happiness**
	+ Happiness comes from being in God and having all things within oneself.
	+ If God were to give all creation to one's soul, it would not be enough to satisfy one's soul.

• **Union with God**
	+ When one is in God, God is in them, and vice versa.
	+ This union is the bare truth, as true as the statement "God is God."

• **Faithful Servant**
	+ God will set the faithful servant over all His goods.
	+ This means that God will give the faithful servant dominion over all creation.

• **The Meaning of "Goods"**
	+ "Goods" refers to the manifoldness of creation.
	+ It also refers to the pure unity that is God Himself.

• **The Unity of God and Humanity**
	+ God and humanity can become one, but this requires the soul to lose its identity.
	+ The Holy Ghost prays for this unity, saying "let them be one as we are one."

• **The Nature of Prayer**
	+ When one prays for something other than God, it is idolatry or unrighteousness.
	+ True prayer is praying in spirit and in truth, without attachment to anything else.

• **The Importance of Love**
	+ Love is the key to union with God.
	+ Those who dwell in love dwell in God, and God dwells in them.

• **The Danger of Idolatry**
	+ Praying for something other than God is a form of idolatry.
	+ This is because it sets up something beside God, making God out of nothing.

• **The Goal of Spiritual Life**
	+ The goal of spiritual life is to attain the love of which Eckhart speaks.
	+ This love is the key to union with God and true happiness.

• **The Role of Jesus Christ**
	+ Jesus Christ is the beloved Lord who helps us attain this love.
	+ He is the one who prays for us to be one with God, as He and the Father are one.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Temple is the Soul**
	+ The Temple is a metaphor for the human soul
	+ God wants to be alone in this temple, which is like Him

• **The Merchants**

	+ They are good people who do good works, but with a wrong intention

	+ They do good works to get something in return from God
	+ They want to barter with God, which is a mistake

• **God's Freedom**
	+ God is not bound to reward good works or gifts
	+ He acts freely and out of love, not because of human actions

• **The Consequences of Merchandising**
	+ God casts out the merchants from the Temple

	+ True freedom comes from being at one with God
	+ A person who is at one with God is perfectly free in their deeds

	+ To enter the Temple, one must do all works solely for God's glory
	+ One must be free of any desire for reward or recognition

• **The Result of True Freedom**
	+ When one acts solely for God's glory, their works are spiritual and godly
	+ The merchants are driven out of the Temple, and God is alone

• **The Importance of Intention**
	+ The intention behind one's actions is crucial
	+ If one's intention is to glorify God, their actions are pure and spiritual

• **The Goal of Spiritual Freedom**
	+ The goal is to be free from all taint of trading and merchandising
	+ To be free to think only of God and God's glory, without any self-interest.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Nature of God and Humanity**
	+ God is entirely free and seeks not His own.

• **The Importance of Freedom**

	+ The soul, on the other hand, is created and cannot return to its created state on its own.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Power of the Father's Heart**
	+ This power is a spiritual energy that flows from the spirit and remains in the spirit.
	+ It is a power that touches neither time nor flesh.

• **The Joy and Glory of God**
	+ In this power, God is ever verdant and flowering in all the joy and glory that He is in Himself.
	+ There is such heartfelt delight and deep joy that none can fully tell of.

• **The Eternal Begetting of the Son**
	+ The eternal Father is ever begetting His eternal Son without pause in this power.
	+ This power jointly begets the Father's Son and itself, this self-same Son.

• **The Reward of Suffering for God**
	+ If a man suffers for God's sake and God alone, his joy would be so great that all suffering and poverty would be insignificant.
	+ He would be all too richly rewarded for all that he had ever endured.

• **The Eternity of the Now**
	+ If a man's spirit were always united with God in this power, he would not age.
	+ The Now in which God made the first man and the last man shall cease to be are the same in God.

• **The Divine Sovereignty**
	+ This power is the divine sovereignty dwelling in the spirit.
	+ It is a power that dwells in one light with God, having no suffering and no sequence of time.

• **Another Power of the Spirit**
	+ There is another power, immaterial and spiritual, flowing from the spirit and remaining in the spirit.
	+ In this power, God is fiery, aglow with all His riches, sweetness, and bliss.

• **The Joy and Bliss of God**
	+ In this power, there is such great joy and vast unmeasured bliss that none can tell of it or reveal it fully.
	+ If a man were to glimpse this bliss and joy, all his sufferings would be a trifle.

• **Knowing Whether Suffering is One's Own or God's**
	+ If suffering hurts and is hard to bear, it is likely one's own suffering.
	+ If suffering does not hurt and is not hard to bear, it is likely suffering for God and God alone.

• **God Bearing the Burden**
	+ If a man suffers purely for God's sake and God alone, God bears the burden.
	+ The burden would not hurt him or bear him down, and God would make it light and pleasant.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **The Nature of the Citadel**
	+ The citadel is a power in the soul that is free and exempt from all names and forms.
	+ It is as one and simple as God, and cannot be glimpsed or understood by humans.
* **The Relationship between God and the Citadel**
	+ God dwells in the power of the citadel and bears His only-begotten Son within it.
	+ The spirit gives birth to the Son and is itself the Son in this light.
* **The Transcendence of the Citadel**
	+ The citadel is elevated above all modes and powers, including God's own modes and properties.
	+ Neither God's power nor His modes can gaze into the citadel.
* **God's Relationship with the Citadel**
	+ God cannot see into the citadel while existing in modes and properties.
	+ To see into the citadel, God would have to leave behind His divine names and personal properties.
* **The Entrance of God into the Citadel**
	+ God can enter the citadel only as one and simple, without mode or properties.
	+ In this sense, God is neither Father, Son, nor Holy Ghost.
* **The Identity of the Soul and God**
	+ In the citadel, the soul is the same as God and not otherwise.
	+ The soul and God are identical in this part of the soul.
* **The Goal of Spiritual Aspiration**
	+ The goal is to become a citadel to which Jesus may ascend and abide eternally.
	+ This requires the soul to be one and simple, like God.
* **The Role of the Will and Intellect**
	+ The will and intellect are involved in the process of spiritual aspiration.
	+ The Franciscans gave the will supremacy, while the Dominicans (and Eckhart) gave the intellect supremacy.
* **The Importance of Spiritual Understanding**
	+ Spiritual understanding is necessary to grasp the nature of the citadel.
	+ The text emphasizes the importance of spiritual insight and experience.
* **The Call to Spiritual Aspiration**
	+ The text concludes with a call to spiritual aspiration and a prayer for God's help.
	+ The goal is to become a citadel to which Jesus may ascend and abide eternally.

• **Christ's knowledge of individuals**

	+ The heavenly Father's power can grasp us in a loving embrace
	+ This is a state of being borne up unknowingly with tempestuous power

• **The goal of spiritual ascent**
	+ The goal of spiritual ascent is to become one with God
	+ This involves seeing God spiritually, free from all images
	+ It is a state of being two, yet one, with God.

• **Peter's Experience**

• **The Third Way**
	+ The third way is being at home, seeing God without means in His own being
	+ Christ is the way, truth, and life, one Person in three aspects
	+ This way is the goal, where the spirit is at rest and united in joyous eternity

• **Martha and Mary**
	+ Martha was "with care" but not "in care", troubled by her lower powers
	+ Mary was "in care", not troubled by her lower powers, and was with God

• **Three Things in Our Works**
	+ Orderly: corresponds to the highest
	+ Understanding: knows nothing temporal that is better
	+ Mindful: feels living truth joyously present in good works

• **Christ's Words to Martha**
	+ Christ said, "You are troubled about many things"

	+ Christ said, "One thing is needful", which is the One that is God

	+ All creatures need God, for if God took back what is His, all creatures would perish
	+ The soul of Christ is united with the eternal Person, and that is the One that is needful

• **Martha's Desire**
	+ Martha wished her sister to be equally established in virtue
	+ Her desire came from a mature ground, wishing her all that pertains to eternal bliss

• **Christ's Response to Martha**
	+ Christ said, "Never fear, Martha, she has chosen the best part"
	+ He said that the best thing that can befall a creature shall be hers: she shall be blessed like Martha

• **The Goal of the Spirit**
	+ The spirit is ever at rest, united in joyous eternity
	+ This is the goal, where the spirit is at peace and united with God

• **The Importance of Unity**
	+ Unity with God is the highest that can happen to us, except the vision of God in His naked nature
	+ This unity is the key to joy and peace, and is the goal of our works and desires

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **Virtuous living depends on the will**
	+ There are three kinds of will: sensible, rational, and eternal
	+ The sensible will seeks guidance and requires a proper teacher
	+ The rational will follows the example of Jesus Christ and the saints
* **The goal of virtuous living**
	+ To resign one's will to God and do what is known to be right
	+ To seek guidance from God and follow His commands
* **The nature of saints**
	+ Saints can be moved by joy and sorrow, but can also remain steadfast in their faith
	+ No saint is completely immune to pain or pleasure, but can remain indifferent to them through divine grace
* **The importance of activity and work**
	+ Activity and work can be turned to eternal profit and help in virtuous living
	+ Martha's activity was not a hindrance to her virtuous living, but rather a means to achieve it
* **The distinction between Martha and Mary**
	+ Martha was a "Martha" in name, but not in essence, until she learned to live virtuously
	+ Mary was a "Mary" in essence, with a well-disciplined body and obedient will
* **The relationship between the will and the senses**
	+ It is impossible to reach a point where sensible things do not affect the senses
	+ However, it is possible to submit to the will and bid the will stand back from the senses
* **The role of insight and understanding**
	+ Insight and understanding can guide the will and lead to virtuous living
	+ The will can submit to insight and bid the will stand back from it, leading to joy and harmony
* **The goal of virtuous living**
	+ To attain a state where the will remains solely with God, saying "Lord, I am thine and thou art mine"
	+ To remain steadfast in faith and virtuous living, even in the face of adversity
* **The importance of divine grace**
	+ Divine grace can help individuals remain indifferent to joy and sorrow
	+ Divine grace can also help individuals remain steadfast in their faith and virtuous living
* **The relationship between the will and God's will**
	+ The will can be in harmony with God's will, leading to virtuous living and eternal bliss
	+ The will can also remain solely with God, saying "Lord, I am thine and thou art mine"

• **The Importance of Good Works**

	+ Not a member of his body but practiced virtues

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Understanding God's will**
	+ God's will is not just about personal salvation or pleasure
	+ If you seek your own good or salvation, you are not God's servant
	+ God's will is about His glory, not just your own profit

• **The meaning of "the Lord my God"**
	+ If God wills something, you should accept it and not resist
	+ If you resist God's will, you are not truly His servant
	+ God's will is not just about your own desires or preferences

• **God's anger and sorrow**
	+ God is angry and sorrowful when we jeopardize our salvation
	+ God's greatest sorrow is the death of His only-begotten Son, Jesus Christ
	+ God's will is not just about His own desires, but about our salvation

• **The power of resignation**
	+ When you resign your will to God, you can "catch" God and bind Him
	+ God will do nothing but what you will, if you have fully surrendered to Him
	+ God's will becomes your own, and He swears to do nothing but what you will

• **The difference between "thy will be done" and true surrender**
	+ Saying "thy will be done" is not the same as truly surrendering to God's will
	+ If your will is God's will, that is good, but if God's will is your will, that is far better
	+ True surrender means accepting God's will, even if it is difficult or painful

• **The union of wills**
	+ When your will is united with God's will, you can bear any pain or suffering
	+ You will not desire to change God's will, even if it is difficult or painful
	+ Your will becomes one with God's will, and you are no longer separate from Him

• **The role of the Holy Spirit**
	+ The Holy Spirit receives his being and becoming from you, as from God
	+ You are in God, and God is in you, when your will is united with His
	+ The Holy Spirit cannot exclude you from God's will, because you are one with Him

• **Moses' example**
	+ Moses' will became fully God's will, and he prioritized God's honor over his own felicity
	+ Moses was willing to be "blotted out of the book of life" if it meant God's honor was preserved
	+ Moses' example shows the importance of surrendering to God's will, even if it is difficult or painful

• **The relationship between God and the righteous**
	+ A righteous person's will becomes one with God's will
	+ God's will becomes the righteous person's own, and they are no longer separate from Him
	+ The righteous person's will is not their own, but God's will, and they are willing to bear any pain or suffering for God's honor

• **The importance of surrender**
	+ Surrendering to God's will is essential for true union with Him
	+ It is not just about saying "thy will be done," but about truly surrendering to God's will
	+ True surrender means accepting God's will, even if it is difficult or painful, and prioritizing His honor over your own desires or preferences.

	+ A master says God became man, raising up humanity
	+ The speaker is skeptical, asking what good it does to have a rich or wise brother if one is poor or foolish

• **The Speaker's Perspective**
	+ God not only became man, but took on human nature
	+ This nature is the source of all goodness in saints, Mary, and Christ

	+ Christ's humanity is the source of all blessedness
	+ This nature is one and simple, yet can be distorted by human mediation

	+ One must be pure of heart, having abolished creatureliness
	+ One must be free of nothing (negation)

	+ The speaker says nothing (negation) burns in hell

	+ The speaker uses a simile to illustrate the nature of hell
	+ The burning coal represents the presence of something that is not in the speaker's hand

• **The Nature of God's Presence**
	+ God and those who stand before Him have something that those separated from God do not
	+ This absence (negation) torments souls in hell more than self-will or other vices

• **The Implications of Union with God**
	+ One who exists in the nakedness of this nature is free from all mediation
	+ One who is pure of heart and free of nothing can experience union with God

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **The Imperfection of "Not"**
	+ The text argues that anything that is not God is imperfect.
	+ To be perfect, one must be rid of "not" and become one with God.
* **The Inner World**
	+ The text distinguishes between the external world and the inner world.
	+ The inner world is where God's ground is one's own ground.

	+ When one lives from their own ground, they live without "why".
	+ This is because they live from their own source, not from external motivations.
* **The Folly of Seeking God through Special Means**
	+ Seeking God through special means, such as meditation or devotion, is misguided.
	+ This approach seeks the way, not the truth, and misses the true nature of God.
* **The Nature of Life**
	+ Life lives from its own ground and gushes forth from its own source.
	+ This is why life lives without "why", because it lives for itself.
* **The Relationship between Creature and God**
	+ When a creaturely image takes shape in one, it deprives God of His fullness.
	+ When the image goes out, God comes in, and God desires to be within one.
* **The Favor of Letting God Be God**
	+ It does no harm to let God be God in one's life.
	+ By going out of oneself, God can come in and fill one's life.
* **The Simple Unity of God**
	+ When one and God have gone out, what is left is one and simple.
	+ In this unity, the Father bears the Son in the inmost source.
* **The Freedom of the Will**
	+ The will is free when it stands untouched by creatures and creation.
	+ When the will turns back to its primal source, it recovers its true birthright of freedom.
* **The Possibility of True Freedom**
	+ The will can never return to its primal source insofar as it is dispersed in time.
	+ However, when the will turns back to its source, it recovers its freedom and all time lost is recovered.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **The Nature of the Divine Image**
	+ The divine image is impressed on the soul's inmost nature
	+ It is the noblest part of the divine nature, patterned in the image of the soul

	+ The image precedes the will and the will follows the image
* **The Relationship Between Image and Nature**
	+ The image is not a mediator between image and nature
	+ Understanding, knowledge, and wisdom are not mediators
	+ The divine image breaks forth from the fecundity of nature without mediation

	+ The Son is the first issue of God's nature and is properly called an image of the Father
	+ The Holy Ghost is not an image, but an efflorescence of the Father and the Son

	+ The image reflects God's nature and all that He is and can perform
	+ God's nature pours out into His image while remaining intact within itself
* **The Image as a Natural Product**
	+ The image is a natural product, like a branch from a tree
	+ It is not created by the will, but breaks forth from the nature of God
* **The Image as Not of Itself or For Itself**
	+ The image is not of itself or for itself, but belongs to the nature of God
	+ It takes its being solely from the nature of God and has one essence with it
* **The Image as a Reflection of God's Essence**
	+ The image reflects God's essence and is the same essence as God
	+ It is not a separate entity, but a part of God's nature
* **The Image as Not a Mediator**
	+ The image is not a mediator between God and humanity
	+ It is not a means by which God communicates with humanity
* **The Importance of Understanding the Image**
	+ Understanding the nature of the divine image is essential for living a virtuous life
	+ It is a key to understanding how to live in accordance with God's will

• **The soul's imperfection**

	+ This is because it is affected by the world and its lower powers.

	+ The Father speaks the Son unspoken, and the Son remains within the Father.

• **God's Outgoing and Ingoing**
	+ God's outgoing is His ingoing, meaning that God's presence is both within and without.
	+ The more one approaches God, the more God speaks Himself within them.

• **Rational Creatures and God**
	+ Rational creatures, like humans, try to speak God in their works, but cannot fully express Him.
	+ God remains unspoken, despite the efforts of rational creatures to express Him.

• **Names and God**
	+ Some names, like "God" and "Lord", are proper to God and exclude all other things.
	+ Other names, like "fatherhood" and "sonhood", imply a reference upward to God and a pointing to time.

• **The Limitations of Names**
	+ Any name we give to God is not God itself, but rather a reference to a aspect of God's nature.
	+ God is above names and above nature, and cannot be fully expressed in words.

• **The Ineffability of God**
	+ God is an unspoken word, and His nature is unspeakable.
	+ Only God Himself can utter the word that is God, and He does so within Himself.

	+ The intellect is even more expansive, allowing the soul to perceive distant places as if they were nearby.

• **The Multitude of Angels**
	+ The number of angels is beyond all numbering and cannot be conceived.
	+ A person who can distinguish without number or quantity would perceive a hundred angels as one.

• **The Nature of Angels**
	+ Each angel has a complete nature, with all the power and wisdom of all men combined.
	+ Angels are distinct from one another, just as different animals have different natures.

• **The Kingdom of God and the Angels**
	+ The multitude of angels proclaims God's kingdom, just as a lord is proclaimed by the number of his knights.
	+ God is known as the "Lord God of Hosts" because of the multitude of angels.

• **The Role of Angels in the Birth of God**
	+ Angels co-operate and help when God is born in the soul.
	+ They have pleasure and joy in the birth, but do not act; God performs the birth alone.

• **The Angel Gabriel**
	+ The angel Gabriel was called to perform a specific task.
	+ No one can know an angel's true name, and Gabriel's name is derived from his work as a messenger.

• **The Soul's Name**
	+ The soul has no true name, just as God has no true name.
	+ The soul is given a name based on its activity or function.

• **The Object of Power**
	+ The object of all the power of nature is to effect the birth of God in the soul.
	+ This birth is a work of power and exertion, where God works powerfully to bring about a new creation.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **God's intention in creation**
	+ To produce a being like Himself
	+ When this is not possible, to produce a being as similar as possible
	+ When power is lacking, to produce a being with some resemblance

• **God's power and nature**
	+ God has plenitude of power
	+ In His birth, He produces a being like Himself
	+ God's birth is a work of power, truth, and wisdom

• **The soul's potential**
	+ The soul can become like God through love and devotion
	+ If the soul loves earthly things, it becomes earthly
	+ If the soul loves God, it becomes like God

• **The nature of God's work in the soul**
	+ God's work in the soul is not hindered by the soul's failings
	+ The soul's hindrances are like worm-eaten wood, not God's fault
	+ God's work in the soul is a birth, not a creation

• **The significance of being "without woe"**
	+ Being without creaturehood means being without woe and hell
	+ The less creaturehood, the less woe
	+ God is God because He is without creaturehood

• **The nature of God's grace**
	+ The least work of God's grace is loftier than all angels in their nature
	+ A work of God's grace is greater than creating a new world
	+ God's grace is without works, just like the birth of the soul

• **The relationship between God's work and the world**
	+ God's work in the soul is the source of all creation
	+ The world is like a chip that falls from a plank, a lightning flash
	+ Everything in the world comes from God's work in the soul

• **The significance of the Father's love**
	+ The Father bears forth His only-begotten Son
	+ From this source, God's grace arises
	+ God's love is the source of all creation and redemption

• **The nature of God's birth**
	+ God's birth is a work of power, truth, and wisdom
	+ God's birth is a birth, not a creation
	+ God's birth is the source of all creation and redemption

• **The significance of being "full of grace"**
	+ Being full of God's grace means being like God
	+ The least work of God's grace is loftier than all angels in their nature
	+ God's grace is without works, just like the birth of the soul

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **Sermon 29: Grace and Virtue**
	+ Discusses the nature of grace and its relationship to God
	+ Explains that grace is not a work, but rather an indwelling and union with God
	+ Cites Psalm 118:20, "I have desired a desire, Lord, for thy justice"
* **The Nature of Grace**
	+ Grace is not a work, but rather a delicate and intimate relationship with God
	+ It is not something that can be earned or achieved through human effort
	+ It is a gift that is given freely by God
* **The Relationship between God and Humanity**
	+ God is not distant or separate from humanity, but rather is intimately connected with us
	+ This connection is not something that can be achieved through human effort, but rather is a gift of God's grace
* **The Importance of Desire**
	+ Desire is a key aspect of the human experience, and is essential for spiritual growth and development
	+ The desire for God's justice and righteousness is a fundamental aspect of the human condition
* **The Nature of Virtue**
	+ Virtue is a pure and abstract concept that is detached from all corporeal things
	+ It is not something that can be achieved through human effort, but rather is a gift of God's grace
* **The Relationship between Virtue and Desire**
	+ A good man desires no praise, but rather desires to be worthy of praise
	+ Virtue is not something that can be achieved through human effort, but rather is a gift of God's grace
* **The Nature of Blessedness**
	+ Blessedness lies in four things: having everything that has essence, having it all undivided with one's whole soul, receiving it in God, and taking it from God Himself
	+ Blessedness is not something that can be achieved through human effort, but rather is a gift of God's grace
* **The Importance of Humility**
	+ A good man should not be sorry if people are angry with him, but rather should be sorry to deserve the anger
	+ Humility is essential for spiritual growth and development
* **The Relationship between God and Humanity**
	+ God is not distant or separate from humanity, but rather is intimately connected with us
	+ This connection is not something that can be achieved through human effort, but rather is a gift of God's grace
* **The Nature of God's Relationship with Humanity**
	+ God's relationship with humanity is one of love and intimacy, rather than distance and separation
	+ This relationship is not something that can be achieved through human effort, but rather is a gift of God's grace

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **To see God, one must be dead**
	+ According to St. Gregory, being dead means being dead to the world
	+ This allows one to be less touched by worldly things and more open to God

• **The intellect is better than the will**
	+ The intellect's work is knowing, and it never rests until it touches the truth
	+ The will's work is desire and love, which are secondary to knowing

• **Desire and love are different**
	+ Desire is about wanting something, while love is about having it
	+ When we have something, desire falls away and love takes over

• **The means to see God are necessary**
	+ Without means, we could see nothing, according to one master
	+ But another master says that without means, we could see an ant in heaven

• **The soul must be purified and refined**
	+ The soul must be strained by light and grace to see God
	+ This purification allows the soul to be borne aloft and prepared for the divine vision

• **The second name, Bar-Jona, means "son of grace"**
	+ This name represents the soul's purification and preparation for the divine vision

• **The third name, Simon, means "that which is obedient"**
	+ To hear God, one must be far removed from people and listen diligently
	+ This obedience allows the soul to be directly exposed to the ray of divine light

• **The soul must be raised up to receive the divine light**
	+ The highest part of the soul must be raised up directly under the ray of divine light
	+ This allows the soul to be fired and inflamed with the love of God

• **The natural mind cannot touch God without means**
	+ The natural mind is not noble enough to touch God without the six things mentioned
	+ These six things are: being dead to all that is unlike, being purified in light and grace, being without means, being obedient to God's word, being subject to the divine light, and living according to the highest power of the soul

• **Blessedness is living according to the highest power of the soul**
	+ This means living according to the soul's highest potential and striving upward
	+ Blessedness is received from God's highest part, and we must keep our souls open to receive it.

• **The Intellect is the Head of the Soul**

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Holy Ghost as the first gift from God**

	+ The intellect is seen as the woman's current "husband", representing her relationship with God
	+ It is a servant that receives and keeps God's presence, but can also ignore God's goodness and power

• **The two sons of the soul (understanding and will)**

	+ This is a secondary interpretation of the "two sons" of the soul

	+ This potentiality is actualized through the birth of the Son, representing the soul's union with God

	+ The two sons of the soul (understanding and will, or potentiality and actuality) are taken by the creditors of the soul (sin and ignorance)

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Mary's boldness**
	+ She asked to see where Jesus' body was carried, showing her boldness and willingness to take risks.
	+ She was not afraid, even though she was a woman and others were afraid.

• **Reasons for her boldness**
	+ She had given herself to Jesus, so she had nothing to lose.
	+ She was inwardly focused on God, not outwardly focused on her surroundings.
	+ Her heart was with Jesus, so she was not afraid.

• **Desire to be killed**
	+ Mary desired to be killed, hoping that her soul would find God in the grave.
	+ She believed that God had broken forth out of man and something of God remained in the grave.

• **Fear of losing the grave**
	+ Mary was afraid that if she left the grave, she would lose it and have nothing left.
	+ She had lost God twice, once on the cross and once in the grave.

• **Why she stood up**
	+ She stood up to see further around, in case God was hidden.
	+ She was inwardly focused on God, so she stood up outwardly.
	+ Her grief was so great that she had no need to sit.

• **Receiving God**
	+ A person standing up is more receptive to God, but humility is also important.
	+ True humility allows God to work in a person's life, making them more receptive to God.

• **Humility and receptivity**
	+ The more a person is sunk in humility, the more receptive they are to God.
	+ Humility is like a cup that can hold more when it is sunk in the ground.

• **Jesus' withdrawal**
	+ Jesus withdrew from Mary for a long time, causing her to be bold and seek him out.
	+ Jesus' withdrawal was a test of Mary's faith and love for him.

• **The nature of God's house**
	+ God's house is a house of prayer, where we praise Him
	+ It is a place of silence and peace, where God speaks to the soul

• **True worship**
	+ True worshippers will worship in the spirit and in truth
	+ We are not truth, but rather true, with some falsehood mixed in

• **The role of the soul**
	+ The soul should stand in the doorway of God's house and pronounce His praise
	+ Everything in the soul should speak His praise, yet none should hear its voice

• **God's joy in the Word**
	+ God begets His Son and has joy in the Word
	+ He utters the Word timelessly, without ceasing

• **What praises God**
	+ Likeness praises God
	+ Whatever is unlike God does not praise God

• **The nature of prayer**
	+ Prayer is a true ascent into God
	+ It is not just words or lip service, but a genuine union with God

• **The conditions for prayer**
	+ We must be holy and collected
	+ We must be one spirit, without division or distraction

• **The soul's ascent**
	+ The soul must rise above all creatures and things
	+ It must transcend all things, including time and space

• **The stages of spiritual growth**
	+ Morning: the light of the soul shines over all creatures
	+ Mid-morning: the light of the angel shines over the light of the soul
	+ The path of the just is like the shining of the morning star

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **The Path to Perfection**
	+ The path to perfection is fair and easy, but it requires the divine light to shine over the angel's light.
	+ When the divine light shines, the day is at its highest and longest, and everything is like God.
* **The Divine Light**
	+ The divine light is the highest and most perfect light, which embraces the angel's light and the soul's light.
	+ When the divine light shines, everything is ordered and erect, and there is nothing that does not praise God.
* **The Soul's Sanctification**
	+ The soul is sanctified when it is united with Christ's salvation and walled round by the divine light.
	+ The soul is sanctified when it excludes all multiplicity and is well established in Christ's salvation.
* **The Holy Ghost**
	+ The Holy Ghost purifies the soul in the light and in grace, drawing it up to the highest.
	+ The Holy Ghost is the source of all holiness, and it is through the Holy Ghost that the soul is sanctified.
* **The Relationship between God and the Soul**
	+ God rests in the soul as much as the soul rests in God.
	+ When the soul rests wholly in God, God rests wholly in the soul.
* **The Likeness of Nature**
	+ Nature works upward from the lowest to the highest, just like the Holy Ghost works in the soul.
	+ The masters say that air becomes fire only when it is rarefied and hot, illustrating the process of spiritual growth.
* **The Rainbow as a Symbol**
	+ The yellow and green in the rainbow merge into one another, illustrating the blending of opposites in nature.
	+ The rainbow is a symbol of the divine likeness and power that is present in all creation.
* **The Highest Angel**
	+ The highest angel is so close to the first effusion (God) that it has within it a great deal of divine likeness and power.
	+ The highest angel is capable of creating the world and all the angels beneath it.
* **The Power of God**
	+ God is so lofty and pure and simple that He causes His highest creature to work by His power.
	+ The power of God is present in all creation, and it is through this power that the highest angel is able to create.
* **The Importance of Likeness**
	+ Likeness is the key to rest and unity, and it is through likeness that the soul is sanctified.
	+ Everything that is like God is within and near by, and it is through likeness that the soul is able to rest in God.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **The Nature of God**
	+ God is described as having three aspects: essence, unity, and goodness
	+ Essence is the Father, unity is the Son with the Father, and goodness is the Holy Ghost
* **The Soul's Journey**
	+ The soul is compared to a sanctified city that is taken by the Holy Ghost into its source, the Son
	+ The Son bears the soul further into its source, the Father, where it finds eternal wisdom
* **The Meaning of Nairn**
	+ Nairn means "son of a dove" and signifies simplicity
	+ It also represents a flood of water, symbolizing the soul's need to be immovable towards sin and imperfection
* **The Role of the Disciples**
	+ The disciples are the divine light that should flow into the soul and flood it with virtues
	+ The soul must ascend with fiery aspiration to surpass the great merits of the angels
* **The Gate of Love and Unity**
	+ The gate through which God melts outward is goodness, while the gate through which God melts inward is unity
	+ The soul must pass through this gate to enter love and unity
* **The Story of the Young Man**
	+ The story of the young man who was raised from the dead by Jesus represents the soul's resurrection
	+ The young man's resurrection symbolizes the soul's ability to stand up and live in the presence of God
* **The Power of Words**
	+ Words have great power and can work wonders
	+ The power of words comes from the first Word, which is the eternal Word of God
* **The Power of Herbs and Stones**
	+ Herbs and stones also have great power, but their power is derived from the likeness wrought in them by the stars and the might of heaven
	+ The soul should arise in its natural light and enter the angelic light, and then the divine light
* **The Goal of the Soul**
	+ The soul's goal is to stand between the three lights at the crossroads, where the eternal Word speaks life into it
	+ The soul becomes living and replies in the Word, achieving unity with God
* **Conclusion**
	+ The sermon concludes with a prayer that the soul may reply in the Word and achieve unity with God.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Story of the Widow and Her Son**
	+ A widow's son is dead, and she comes to Jesus
	+ Jesus says, "Young man, stand up!" (Luke 7:14)
	+ This story is an allegory for the soul's journey

• **Interpretation of the Allegory**
	+ The widow represents the understanding
	+ The husband represents the man in the soul
	+ The son represents the highest intellect

• **The Role of the Intellect**
	+ The intellect is the highest part of the soul
	+ It receives divine light and becomes self-knowing
	+ It knows God and is the source of all understanding

• **The Debate between Understanding and Love**
	+ Some say understanding is higher, others say love is higher
	+ Understanding says it is born in clear light and can understand itself
	+ Love says it is the goodness that God is Himself

• **The Relationship between Understanding and Love**
	+ Understanding says it is the servant of love
	+ Love says it is eternal and must stay with understanding
	+ The highest intellect says it needs no one and stands before its source

• **The Meaning of "Young Man, Stand Up!"**
	+ The phrase means that the intellect has reached its highest point
	+ It forgets all that helped it in its ascent and stands alone

• **The Soul's Journey**
	+ The soul begins to rise up, and the man in the soul is reborn
	+ The old habits are gone, and the soul stands in its primal innocence

• **The Transcendence of the Soul**
	+ The soul transcends angelic being and becomes a new being
	+ It stands in its true being and is no longer bound by old habits

• **The Role of Faith and Hope**
	+ Faith and hope must stay behind, as they cannot go further
	+ The soul must let go of all created things and stand before its source

• **The Final Realization**
	+ The highest intellect realizes it needs no one and stands alone
	+ It knows itself and God, and all created things must remain behind

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **The Man in the Soul**
	+ The man in the soul is one who has accomplished spiritual growth and no longer needs further help.
	+ God works in him and knows him as He knows Himself.
	+ He is loved by God and performs all works.
* **Characteristics of the Man in the Soul**
	+ He is empty of all things and has gained all that Christ had by nature.
	+ His body is fully suffused with the noble essence of the soul.
	+ He is a man divine, with a divine essence.
* **Pity and Recognition**
	+ These people are strangers and unknown to others.
	+ Only those with the same light can truly recognize them.
	+ Those on the way to spiritual growth may recognize them in part.
* **Value of the Man in the Soul**
	+ He is worth more than all the riches in the world.
	+ God is with him with all His power.
	+ He is not to be compared to sinners.
* **The Good Man and Sin**
	+ Whatever the good man eats or drinks bears him up in Christ to the Father.
	+ Sinners, on the other hand, are drawn down to sin by what they eat or drink.
* **Christ's Presence**
	+ Christ is with those who are in true unity with their soul and body.
	+ This is the meaning of "Where two are gathered together in my name I will be with them" (Matt. 18:20).
* **The Perfected Ones**
	+ They are hard to recognize, as they do not follow the same practices as others.
	+ They need nothing, as they are in possession of their eternal bliss.
	+ They perform the most valuable work, which is inward and spiritual.
* **The Kingdom of the Perfected Ones**
	+ It is blessed to dwell in the kingdom of one such person.
	+ They do more eternal good in an instant than all outward works.
* **Recognizing and Loving the Perfected Ones**
	+ We should recognize and love them, and enter into possession of the city they have won.
	+ This is the key to spiritual growth and eternal bliss.
* **Conclusion**
	+ We should seek to recognize and love the perfected ones, and enter into the city of our true birthright.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Divine Light in the Soul**
	+ The divine light breaks forth in the soul, filling it with pure and perfect day
	+ This light is hotter than midday, as it encompasses and stores up the heat of the day
	+ The soul's rest is not just a physical state, but a spiritual one, where everything is filled with divine light

• **The Meaning of Evening**
	+ Evening in the soul is when the light of the world fades, and a person is indrawn and rests
	+ This is a time of spiritual rest, where the soul is filled with divine light and peace

• **The Place of the Soul**
	+ The Godhead is the place of the soul, and is nameless
	+ The soul's rest is in this nameless place, where all things have been God in God

• **The Nature of God**
	+ God is unspoken, and cannot be fully expressed in words
	+ St. Augustine says that God is not unspoken, but rather more silence than speech

• **The Importance of Silence**
	+ Our ancient masters believed that silence was the best way to express the truth about God
	+ They preferred to be silent and point with their finger, rather than speak of things that could not be fully expressed

• **God as Spirit**
	+ We say that God is a spirit, but this is not entirely accurate
	+ St. Gregory says that we cannot truly speak of God, and that what we say is just a stammering attempt

• **The Location of Creatures**
	+ All creatures bud and blossom in due order from the ground of the nameless place
	+ The location of the soul proceeds from this ground, and is a reflection of the divine order

• **The Will and Rest**
	+ Whoever rests in God does so without their own volition
	+ The will is free, taking nothing from matter, and is unpracticed in the sense that it is not bound by physical limitations

• **The Place of the Soul and the Will**
	+ The will is not a separate entity from the soul, but rather a part of it
	+ The will is free to rest in God, and is not bound by physical limitations

• **The Relationship Between the Soul and God**
	+ The soul's rest is in the nameless place, where all things have been God in God
	+ This rest is a reflection of the divine order, and is a time of spiritual peace and tranquility.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Knowledge vs. Wisdom**
	+ Knowledge is free, but it takes from matter and corporeal things
	+ Wisdom is also free, but it is not bound to the five senses
	+ The soul has within it all knowledge, and outward practices serve to awaken it

• **The Role of the Senses**
	+ The senses are involved in the preparation for wisdom
	+ The soul is awakened and the image of knowledge is imprinted through the senses
	+ The senses are merely an exercising of the senses, not the source of wisdom

• **The Nature of Wisdom**
	+ Wisdom is a state of repose and divine love
	+ It is the highest and purest part of the soul
	+ It is not bound by time or space, but is eternal and universal

• **The Day of the Soul**
	+ The day of the soul is a state of being, not a temporal concept
	+ It has its own morning, midday, and evening, which are not sequential
	+ The soul remains in the light of God and in the silence of pure repose

• **The Place of God**
	+ God is the place and position of all things
	+ He is the natural place of all creatures
	+ He is the subtler and more powerful element, operating in all things

• **The Elements and Their Places**
	+ The elements are ranked in terms of their subtlety and power
	+ The air is the place of water and the earth
	+ The fire is the place of the air, and so on

• **The Nature of the Elements**
	+ The elements are not places in themselves, but are part of a larger whole
	+ The earth is too coarse to be a place, but water is in part a place
	+ The more powerful an element is, the more it can operate in things beneath it

• **Jacob's Rest**
	+ Jacob rested in the evening, when the sun had gone down
	+ He wanted to rest in the place of God, which is the natural place of all creatures

• **The Evening of the Day**
	+ The evening of the day is the hottest time, when the heat of midday is added to it
	+ It is a state of repose and divine love, where the soul is thoroughly penetrated and made incandescent

• **The Significance of the Text**
	+ The text is a commentary on the Gospel of John, chapter 20, verse 19
	+ It is a sermon that explores the nature of wisdom, the role of the senses, and the place of God in the world.

	+ This violence is also done to ourselves, as far as we are able

	+ We must be made ready to receive this gift through humility

	+ The perfection of blessedness lies in both knowledge and love
	+ Knowledge and love are essential for understanding God

• **The Meaning of "A Little"**
	+ The phrase "A little, and you will not see me" has four meanings
	+ Each meaning is distinct, but all convey the importance of humility

• **First Meaning: Seeing God in Nothing**
	+ When we see nothing, we see God (St. Augustine)
	+ This means that we must let go of our attachments and see God in all things

• **Second Meaning: Transcending World and Time**
	+ Unless we transcend world and time, we will not see God
	+ This means that we must see the world and time as small things

• **Third Meaning: Sin and Alien Impressions**
	+ As long as sin or anything akin to sin adheres to the soul, we will not see God
	+ Heaven receives no alien impressions, and we must be pure to see God

• **The Importance of Humility**
	+ Humility is essential for receiving God's gift and seeing Him
	+ We must let go of our attachments and see God in all things to achieve true understanding and love.

• **The Soul's Limitations**
	+ The soul can only do things in the body it is allotted to.
	+ No soul can do anything outside of its own body.

• **The Soul's Knowledge of God**

	+ The fourth sense is opposite to the first three, which are limited.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **Sermon 41**
	+ The sermon is a better version of Pfeiffer's Sermon 41, with variants from the Basle Tauler edition of 1521.
	+ The author is likely Gonsalvus, a Franciscan who debated Eckhart in Paris in 1302 or 1303.
* **The Nature of God**
	+ The Father knows us as far as we know Him, and He knows Himself.
	+ Our Lord says that eternal life is knowing Him alone as true God.
* **The Difficulty of Knowing God**
	+ Anything, however small, adhering to the soul prevents us from seeing God.
	+ St. Augustine asked what eternal life was and said that eternal life itself is the answer.
* **The Relationship between God and Humanity**
	+ No one knows better what eternal life is than eternal life itself.
	+ Our Lord Jesus Christ is the Eternal Life, and knowing Him is eternal life.
* **The Importance of Direct Experience**
	+ If a man saw God from afar, he would not depart from God for an instant.
	+ Direct experience of God is more important than any other experience.
* **The Role of Scripture**
	+ The sermon quotes St. John's Gospel and other scriptural texts to support its themes.
	+ The author uses scripture to express his meaning, often in a free and interpretive way.
* **The Influence of Neoplatonism**
	+ The sermon reflects Aristotelian and Neoplatonist ideas about the nature of God and the universe.
	+ The author refers to the substantiae separatae of Neoplatonism, which are often identified with angels.
* **The Use of Imagery**
	+ The sermon uses imagery and metaphor to describe the relationship between God and humanity.
	+ The author uses the image of a "little imagelet" to describe the subtle and abstract nature of God.
* **The Importance of Purity**
	+ The sermon emphasizes the importance of purity and simplicity in knowing God.
	+ The author says that anything, however small, adhering to the soul prevents us from seeing God.
* **The Author's Style**
	+ The author's style is characterized by a free and interpretive use of scripture.
	+ The sermon is an example of the author's ability to use language and imagery to express complex theological ideas.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **God's greatness is beyond human comprehension**
	+ God is so vast and wide that human understanding cannot grasp it
	+ Comparing God to the universe is like comparing a needle prick to the entire universe

• **The soul must be stripped of all means to see God**
	+ As long as the soul has any creaturehood or attachment, it cannot see God
	+ The soul must "leap over" all creatures to find God

	+ God's love for the soul is so great that it is a wonder
	+ God's love is His life and being, and the Holy Ghost blossoms forth from it

• **The soul is a very important thing**
	+ The soul is so important that God loves it mightily
	+ The soul's worth is not diminished by its smallness compared to God

• **The eye can see an ant in the sky without a medium**
	+ If there were no medium between the eye and the ant, it could see it
	+ This is because God has no medium and brooks no intervention

• **The soul can see God without a medium**
	+ If the soul were stripped of all means, it could see God
	+ God would appear stripped and bare before the soul

• **Means and mediums block the soul's vision of God**
	+ Joy, fear, expectation, hope, and pain are all means that block the soul's vision of God
	+ These means must be cast off to see God clearly

• **The soul is a mirror that reflects images**
	+ The soul sees images of things, but not the things themselves
	+ The soul sees images without a medium or another image

• **Images are not means, but rather the result of means**
	+ An image is the result of a means, but not another means itself
	+ The soul sees images without a medium or another image

• **The soul must be clarified and rendered subtle to see God**
	+ The soul must be clarified and rendered subtle to see God
	+ This is because God is a spiritual reality that cannot be seen through physical means.

• **The Nature of the Soul**
	+ The soul has a power called the intellect that allows it to grasp God
	+ This power is the medium and image of God, allowing the soul to know God immediately and without any image

• **The Five Properties of the Intellect**
	+ Detached from here and now (place and time)
	+ Like nothing (unlike any other being)

	+ Active and seeking in itself (ever inwardly seeking)
	+ An image (a likeness of God)

	+ "Here and now" refers to place and time, which must be transcended to see God
	+ Everything that touches or smacks of time must go, including the present moment

• **Being Like Nothing**
	+ God is a being that nothing is like and nothing can become like
	+ The intellect is like God in that it is also unlike anything else
	+ All creatures strive to become like God, and nature seeks God in all things

• **Purity and Uncompoundedness**
	+ God can tolerate no mingling or admixture, and neither can the intellect
	+ The soul should be entirely without admixture, like a person who clings to nothing

• **Inward Seeking**
	+ God abides in the innermost, and the intellect goes ever seeking within
	+ The will goes out to seek what it loves, but the intellect seeks within

	+ We can dwell on the way or in the approaches to God, but not in God Himself

• **The Nature of God**

	+ God is a being that can tolerate no mingling or admixture

• **The Human Condition**
	+ Nature secretly and inmost parts seeks and aims at God
	+ All creatures strive to become like God, and nature seeks God in all things

• **Sermon Overview**
	+ The sermon discusses the nature of God and the relationship between God and humanity.
	+ The sermon emphasizes the importance of understanding the concept of "image" in relation to God.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Sermon Fifty: Illumination of Jerusalem**
	+ Based on Isaiah 60:1, "Stand up, Jerusalem, arise and be illumined!"
	+ Can be understood in three senses: prayer for God's grace, spiritual growth, or humility

• **The Soul's Three Powers**
	+ Resemble the Trinity: memory, intelligence, and will
	+ Memory is a secret, hidden art (denotes the Father)
	+ Intelligence is ever-presence/knowing/wisdom (denotes the Son)
	+ Will is a flooding of the Holy Ghost (denotes the Holy Spirit)

• **Alternative View of the Soul's Three Powers**
	+ Some masters divide the soul into three powers: angry power, intelligence, and will
	+ Angry power is compared to the Father and wages war against evil
	+ Anger blinds the soul, but love conquers the senses

• **Location of the Soul's Powers**
	+ Memory works in the liver
	+ Intelligence works in the heart
	+ Will works in the brain

• **God's Implantation of Natural Striving**
	+ God has implanted in the soul a natural striving for the highest good
	+ The first power (memory) strives for the highest good
	+ The second power (intelligence) is satisfied with nothing but the best
	+ The third power (will) is satisfied with nothing but one good

• **God's Resting Place**
	+ God does not rest where He is a beginning of all being (the highest good)
	+ God rests where He is an end and a beginning of all being (the lowest good)

• **Jerusalem as a Height**
	+ Jerusalem means a height
	+ To that which is high, one says "Come down"
	+ To that which is low, one says "Come up"

• **God's Humility**
	+ When we humble ourselves, God comes down from above and enters us
	+ God drives us into one corner and presses His power into us, making us fruitful

• **The Highest Flows into the Lowest**
	+ The highest flows into the lowest
	+ The star above the sun is nobler than the sun and illuminates it
	+ The sun's light is not supreme, but comes from the star above

• **The Humble Person**
	+ A truly humble person subjects all creatures to himself and subjects himself to God
	+ God pours Himself out fully into the humble person, making them fruitful

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **The Root of Sin and Virtue**
	+ Pride is the root of all sins and defects
	+ Humility is the root of all good
* **The Nature of Humility**
	+ Humility is a state of being that allows one to be connected to God
	+ The truly humble man has no need to pray to God, as he can command God
* **The Relationship between Humility and God**
	+ The humble man and God are one
	+ The humble man has as much power over God as God has over Himself
* **The Inwardness of God**
	+ God's height lies in the lowliness of the humble man
	+ God is brought down inwardly, so that the humble man can be raised up
* **The Birth of the Sons of God**
	+ Those who receive God are given the power to become the sons of God
	+ They are born of God, not from flesh and blood, but inwardly
* **The Relationship between the Humble Man and God**
	+ The humble man is not satisfied to be born as the only-begotten Son
	+ He wants to be the Father and to enter into the same equality of eternal paternity
* **The Unity of the Humble Man and God**
	+ The humble man can turn away from God, but God will always wait for him
	+ God is always present in the humble man, and is his own
* **The Gift of God's Godhead**
	+ God gives His Godhead to the man who makes himself His own
	+ Whatever the humble man performs, God performs
* **The Nature of God's Presence**
	+ God is not only a beginning of all our acts and being, but also an end and a repose
	+ God is the light that shines in the darkness, and is not comprehended by it
* **The Lesson of Humility**
	+ We can learn the lesson of humility from Jesus Christ
	+ May God the Father, Son, and Holy Ghost help us all to achieve humility.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **Sermon Fifty-One: Homo Quidam Nobilis Abiit**
	+ The sermon is based on Luke 19:12, "There was a nobleman who went of his own accord into foreign parts and returned home richer."
* **The Nobleman's Journey**
	+ The nobleman represents the soul that has abandoned self and followed God's will.
	+ To follow God's will, one must submit to God with one's desire and heart, and have no regard for created things.
* **Goodness and the Soul**
	+ Goodness in itself does not calm the soul, but rather entices it and looks out.
	+ Good preparedness for everything that is good is fellowship, and grace remains with the desire.
* **God's Will and the Soul**
	+ If God gave the speaker anything outside of His will, they would disregard it.
	+ The least thing that God gives the speaker within His will makes them blessed.
* **The Source of All Goodness**
	+ All creatures have flowed out of God's will.
	+ If the speaker were able to desire God's goodness alone, the Holy Ghost would flow out of it without means.
* **The Unity of God's Goodness**
	+ God's will has savor for the speaker only in the unity where the repose of God's goodness is in all creatures.
	+ This is the final end, where the Holy Ghost is in union with God's goodness alone.
* **The Return of the Nobleman**
	+ The nobleman returns richer than when he set forth, having found himself and all things in the present "now" of unity.
	+ He returns much nobler than when he departed, having abandoned multiplicity and found simplicity.
* **The Sun and God's Humility**
	+ The sun corresponds to God in His highest part and in His unfathomable depth in the depth of His humility.
	+ The humble man has no need to pray for it, for it belongs to him.
* **The Nobleman's Freedom**
	+ The nobleman now dwells in unhampered freedom and pure nakedness, needing to undertake and take on nothing small or great.
	+ Whatever belongs to God belongs to him.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Humble Man is Divine**
	+ The humble man has as much power over God as he has over himself
	+ The humble man is one with God, and all good things are his own
	+ God would have to come to the humble man in hell, and hell would become heaven for him

• **Humility is a Root of the Godhead**
	+ Humility is a virtue that is implanted in the ground of the Godhead
	+ It has its being solely in the eternal One, and nowhere else
	+ The truly humble man is not harmed or led astray by anything

• **The Nature of Man**
	+ Aristotle defines man as a being to whom form is added, giving him being and life
	+ Man understands the images and forms of all creatures with distinctions
	+ This is the highest quality Aristotle can ascribe to a man

• **The Definition of Man**
	+ I define man as a being to whom substance has been added, giving him being, life, and rational being
	+ A rational man is one who understands himself rationally and is detached from all matter and form

• **Detachment and Understanding**
	+ The more a man is detached from all things, the more clearly and rationally he knows all things within himself
	+ Detachment comes from simplicity, and the more pure and simple a man is, the more simply he will understand all multiplicity

• **The Simple Intellect**
	+ The simple intellect is pure and clear in itself, and comprehends the pure bare divine being immediately
	+ It receives divine nature equally with the angels, and this understanding is a great joy

• **The Detached Spirits**
	+ Aristotle speaks of detached spirits in his book Metaphysics
	+ These spirits are not the form of anything, and derive their being by immediate outpouring from God

• **The Nature of the Detached Spirits**
	+ They are not the form of anything, and have no matter or form
	+ They derive their being by immediate outpouring from God, and are not contained in any other being

• **The Joy of the Detached Spirits**
	+ The detached spirits have great joy in understanding the pure bare divine being
	+ This understanding is a great marvel, and would be worth spending a thousand years in hell to experience

• **The Relationship between Man and God**
	+ The humble man is one with God, and all good things are his own
	+ God would have to come to the humble man in hell, and hell would become heaven for him

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **The Nature of God**
	+ God is a pure, naked being that is beyond human comprehension.
	+ This being is the highest concept that can be conceived, according to Aristotle.
* **The Trinity**
	+ The Father is the beginning of the Godhead, comprehending Himself in Himself.
	+ The eternal Word proceeds from the Father, yet remains within.
	+ The Holy Ghost proceeds from both the Father and the Word, yet remains within.
* **The Relationship between the Beginning and the End**
	+ The beginning exists for the sake of the end.
	+ The final end of being is the darkness or nescience of the hidden Godhead.
	+ This darkness comprehends the light of God, but is not illuminated by it.
* **The Nature of God's Relationship with Humanity**
	+ God is a hidden God, beyond human comprehension.
	+ The more we seek God, the less we find Him.
	+ We must seek God in such a way that we never find Him, in order to remain in Him.
* **The Goal of Spiritual Seeking**
	+ The goal of spiritual seeking is to remain in God eternally.
	+ This requires a deep understanding of the nature of God and the self.
* **The Role of the Soul**
	+ The soul is the entire house, which must be illuminated by God.
	+ A good housewife (the soul) must not eat her bread in idleness, but must be active in seeking God.
* **The Relationship between the Soul and God**
	+ The soul and God share a common ground, which is the ground of the soul.
	+ This ground is the source of all being and life.
* **The Importance of Mystical Union**
	+ Mystical union with God is the ultimate goal of spiritual seeking.
	+ This union is a deep and intimate relationship between the soul and God.
* **The Role of Reason and Intellect**
	+ Reason and intellect are essential for understanding the nature of God and the self.
	+ However, they must be used in a way that transcends their limitations.
* **The Importance of Humility and Obedience**
	+ Humility and obedience are essential for spiritual growth and union with God.
	+ We must be willing to surrender our will to God's will, in order to remain in Him.

* **The Soul's Nature**
	+ The soul is created between eternity and time
	+ Eternity represents God, while time represents change and multiplicity
	+ The soul's higher powers touch eternity, while her lower powers are subject to time

* **The Soul's Interaction with the World**
