Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **The Three Powers of the Soul**
	+ The first power is knowledge, which allows the soul to understand God's truth
	+ The second power is irascibilis, or the upward striving power, which helps the soul to strive for God
	+ The third power is the interior will, which draws God's love into the soul
* **Knowledge**
	+ Knowledge is the power to understand God's truth
	+ When the soul gives itself to knowledge, it becomes a light and is united with God
	+ Faith carries the soul where it cannot go with its senses and powers
* **Irascibilis (Upward Striving Power)**
	+ This power strives aloft and cannot tolerate anything above it
	+ If the soul glances aside, it falls into pride, which is sin
	+ This power is called hope, which is a divine virtue
* **Interior Will**
	+ The interior will is turned to face God in the divine will
	+ It draws God's love into the soul, and the soul is drawn by God
	+ This is called divine love, which is another divine virtue
* **Perfection of the Soul**
	+ The soul's perfection lies in knowledge and apprehension of God
	+ It also lies in the union of perfect love
* **Sin**
	+ Sin is turning away from felicity and virtue
	+ Every blessed soul must strive to follow the paths of virtue
* **The Virtuous Soul**
	+ The virtuous soul is clothed with strength to withstand imperfection
	+ It is adorned with truth and worships true poverty
	+ It transcends self and scorns the scorn of men
* **The Virtuous Soul's Desire**
	+ The virtuous soul's desire is to wash and tend sick and filthy people with a pure heart
	+ It flees to God when its outward comforts fail
* **God's Felicity**
	+ God's felicity lies in three things: knowledge, liberty, and complete satisfaction
	+ The soul's perfection lies in knowledge and apprehension of God, and in the union of perfect love
* **Conclusion**
	+ May God help us to illuminate the paths of our house and not eat our bread in idleness.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Introduction to the Sermon**
	+ The sermon is based on the Latin text "AVE GRATIA PLENA DOMINUS TECUM" from the Gospel of Luke (Luke 1:28)
	+ The text is translated to "Hail to thee, full of grace, the Lord is with thee!"

• **Interpretation of the Angel's Greeting**
	+ The angel's greeting is a sign of the humility of the angelic nature
	+ The angel knew himself unworthy to name God's mother
	+ The angel addressed not just Mary, but a great multitude of good souls who desire God

• **Mary's Spiritual Birth**
	+ Mary bore God spiritually before he was born physically
	+ This is a key point in the sermon, emphasizing the importance of spiritual birth

• **The Value of Spiritual Birth**
	+ It is more valuable to God to be born spiritually of an individual virgin or good soul than to be born physically of Mary
	+ This is because we are all children of God, born spiritually, and this is a greater gift than physical birth

• **The Father's Eternal Love**
	+ We are an only son, eternally born of the Father
	+ The Father begot all creatures, and we flowed forth with them while remaining within the Father

• **The Nature of Creation**
	+ The primal images of all creatures are in the Father
	+ This is like a rational image of a piece of wood, which is pure reason

• **The Greatest Good God Did for Man**
	+ The greatest good God did for man was to become man himself
	+ This is the central theme of the sermon

• **The Story of the Rich Man and Lady**
	+ A rich man and lady are the main characters in a story that illustrates God's love for humanity
	+ The lady loses an eye, and the man puts out one of his own eyes to show her that he loves her

• **The Significance of God's Incarnation**
	+ God's incarnation is a sign of his love for humanity
	+ This is the ultimate expression of God's love for us

• **The Purpose of Creation**
	+ All creatures strive to bring forth and emulate the Father
	+ Every active cause works solely for the sake of its end, to find rest and peace in its end.

• **The Nature of God and the Soul**
	+ God creates all creatures, but first begets something uncreated that contains the images of all creatures.
	+ This uncreated spark is akin to God and contains images of all creatures, both above and below.

• **The Relationship Between God and the Soul**
	+ The soul is an only son begotten by God, not by way of being unborn.
	+ The soul is eternally begotten by God in the hidden darkness of eternal concealment.

• **The Process of Begetting**
	+ God begets the soul in the highest part of the soul.
	+ In the same moment, the soul bears the begotten one back into the Father.

• **The Nature of God's Goodness**
	+ God's goodness is poured out superabundantly into all creatures.
	+ This goodness is what makes all creatures alike in their original pure nature.

• **The Relationship Between God and Humanity**
	+ God is unable to withhold His goodness from a humble man.
	+ God pours Himself out into the humble man, giving Himself most and wholly.

• **The Nature of Love**
	+ All sorrow and joy come from love.
	+ God's goodness is His love, and His love is what He gives to humanity.

• **The Fear of God**
	+ A man should not fear God, as this fear is harmful.
	+ The right sort of fear is the fear of losing God's love.

• **The Universality of God's Love**
	+ All creatures, including angels and blades of grass, are one in their original pure nature.
	+ This pure nature is the plenitude of all purity, and it is what makes all creatures alike.

• **The Process of Spiritual Growth**
	+ A man can come to compel God by humbling himself.
	+ When a man humbles himself, God pours Himself out into that man.

• **The Experience of Love**
	+ A man can become wet with love, as the speaker has experienced.
	+ This love is what brings joy and sorrow, and it is what God gives to humanity.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Sermon Fifty-Four**
	+ Based on Matthew 14:22, where Jesus tells his disciples to go up into a little boat and cross the turbulent sea.
	+ The sermon explores the theme of rising above worldly things to hear the Word of God.

• **Rising Above Worldly Things**
	+ To hear the Word and be a disciple of Christ, one must rise above all corporeal things.
	+ This means transcending the turbulence of the mutability of all transitory things.

• **Understanding Natural Things**
	+ To understand natural things, one must strip their understanding bare of all extraneous matters.
	+ This means letting go of worldly attachments and distractions.

• **The Holy Ghost**
	+ The Holy Ghost lifts the soul and supports her, drawing her up with him.
	+ When the Holy Ghost falls away from the soul, she sinks down, but when he is present, she ascends.

• **The Importance of Transcendence**
	+ It is necessary for man to have trodden under foot all things that are of the earth.
	+ This means letting go of worldly attachments and distractions, so that nothing remains but what is akin to the understanding.

• **The Soul's Ascent**
	+ The soul that has transcended all things is lifted and supported by the Holy Ghost.
	+ This soul is borne with him into the ground whence he emanated, into her eternal image.

• **The Image of the Father**
	+ The soul is borne into the image after which the Father has shaped all things.
	+ This image is one in which all things are but one, and in which all things shall again find their end.

• **The Vastness and Profundity of God**
	+ The soul is borne into the vastness and profundity of God, where all things shall again find their end.
	+ This is the ultimate goal of the soul's ascent.

• **The Role of the Holy Ghost**
	+ The Holy Ghost is the one who lifts the soul and supports her, drawing her up with him.
	+ He is the one who bears her into her eternal image, and into the image of the Father.

• **The Importance of Spiritual Growth**
	+ To come to this ultimate goal, one must first have trodden under foot all things that are unlik.
	+ This means letting go of worldly attachments and distractions, and rising above them to hear the Word of God.

• **The Nature of God**
	+ God is a unified, pure being
	+ God is not goodness, being, truth, or one, but rather something beyond these concepts

	+ Love accepts God because He is good
	+ Love is not based on attributes like goodness, being, or truth, but rather on God's pure being
	+ Love is the means by which the soul is united with God

• **The Nature of Truth**
	+ Truth is not an attribute of God, but rather something that comes from God
	+ Truth is not something that can be grasped or understood, but rather something that is revealed
	+ Truth is not a concept, but rather a reality that is experienced

• **The Three Heavens**
	+ The first heaven is detachment from bodily things
	+ The second heaven is estrangement from imagery
	+ The third heaven is a bare understanding in God without intermediaries

	+ He was locked in the embrace of the Godhead
	+ He was aware of his surroundings, even in the third heaven

	+ The soul is capable of being inborne into God
	+ The soul is united with God through love and the Holy Ghost

	+ Clarity is the result of the soul's intellect being established in God
	+ Clarity is the bridge between the soul and God

• **The Relationship Between God and the Soul**
	+ The soul is in-formed in God
	+ The soul is united with God through love and the Holy Ghost
	+ The soul is aware of its surroundings, even in the third heaven

• **The Gift of Being a Disciple of Jesus**
	+ Being a disciple of Jesus is a great gift
	+ It involves being transfigured and formed in the image of God
	+ It involves being united with God through love and the Holy Ghost

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **St. Paul's Experience**
	+ St. Paul's soul remained in his body while his spirit was transported to a higher realm.
	+ He understood eternal things timelessly, not through his lower senses.
* **Understanding Time and Eternity**
	+ St. Paul understood outside of time, not from created angels in time.
	+ He understood from God, who is beyond time and comprehends all things.
* **God's Relationship with St. Paul**
	+ God understood in St. Paul, not the other way around.
	+ The relationship is like the sun shining through glass, where the sun's power is the source of the effect.
* **The Soul's Relationship with the Body**
	+ Association with the body prevents the soul from understanding as clearly as the angels.
	+ The soul knows from without, while God understands within Himself.
* **The Nature of God's Understanding**
	+ God is the source of all things and understands within Himself.
	+ God's understanding is not limited by material things.
* **The Importance of Translucency**
	+ The translucency of St. Paul's soul allowed him to receive divine contemplation.
	+ This translucency is a result of the soul's ability to receive love from the Godhead.
* **The Relationship between God and Humanity**
	+ God made the poor for the rich and the rich for the poor.
	+ Lending to God will result in repayment, but only if one truly believes in God.
* **The Nature of Faith**
	+ Believing in God is a greater thing than believing God.
	+ Faith involves trusting in God's promises, not just seeking personal gain.
* **The Importance of Letting Go**
	+ Giving up all things will result in a hundredfold return.
	+ However, seeking a hundredfold return for personal gain will result in nothing.
* **The Goal of Spiritual Pursuit**
	+ The goal of spiritual pursuit is not to find knowledge, understanding, or devotion, but to find God.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Seeking God's will is key**
	+ Seeking knowledge, understanding, or inwardness is not enough
	+ Seeking God's will alone is the most important thing

• **God's will is all that matters**
	+ All things are pleasing to God and perfect when they are in His will
	+ Outside of God's will, all things are naught and imperfect

• **Pray for God's will alone**
	+ Praying for anything else will get you nothing
	+ Praying for God's will alone will get you everything

• **God is one and indivisible**
	+ Seeking anything but God's will is seeking a fraction, not the whole
	+ God's will is the only thing that is truly one and indivisible

• **Accept what comes from God's will**
	+ Whatever flows from God's will is a gift from God
	+ You don't need to worry about whether it's by nature or grace

• **Lead an ordinary Christian life**
	+ You don't need to do anything special to follow God's will
	+ Just live a simple, ordinary life and trust in God's love

• **Don't question God's will**
	+ If you're sick, it's God's will
	+ If you're suffering, it's God's will
	+ Just accept what happens and trust in God's love

• **Praise God in all circumstances**
	+ Praise God when things are going well
	+ Praise God when things are going badly
	+ Trust in God's love and goodness in all circumstances

• **Seek God's counsel in difficult times**
	+ If something is repugnant to you, seek God's counsel
	+ Bending humbly before God, accept what happens with quiet faith

• **Let God guide your conscience**
	+ Refuse to pay attention to casual happenings
	+ Give up your will wholly to God and accept all things equally from Him

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **God's Nature**
	+ God's nature is not something that can be seen or understood.
	+ God's nature is higher than human nature, and He is more willing to give than humans are to receive.

• **Righteousness and God**
	+ A righteous person has no need for God, as they have already achieved what is necessary.
	+ A righteous person serves God out of love, not out of obligation or need.

• **Progress in the Good Life**
	+ Progress in the good life is not measured by outward works or fasting.
	+ A sure sign of growth is a waxing love for eternal things and a waning interest in temporal things.

• **Despising Oneself for God's Sake**
	+ It is greater to despise and naught oneself for God's sake than to perform great deeds for Him.
	+ A person should turn their will towards God and act without worrying about whether they are doing the right thing.

• **Following One's Will**
	+ Following one's will without questioning is necessary for progress.
	+ If one were to plan every step, they would get nowhere.

• **Fear Not Those Who Would Kill the Body**
	+ Those who would kill the body cannot kill the soul.
	+ The soul is not killed by external forces, but by internal ones.

• **The Nature of Man**
	+ The noblest thing in man is his blood when it wills good.
	+ The most evil thing in man is his blood when it wills evil.

• **The Relationship Between Flesh and Blood**
	+ When the blood rules the flesh, a person is humble, patient, and chaste.
	+ When the flesh rules the blood, a person is haughty, angry, and lascivious.

• **God's Creation**
	+ God did not work when He created heaven and earth, as there was no work in Him.
	+ God's creation is a likeness of Himself, and He works in the soul to create a work that is nothing but love.

• **God's Nature and Love**
	+ God loves Himself and His nature, being, and Godhead.
	+ God savors Himself and all creatures, not as creatures but as God.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **God's Nature**
	+ God is loftier than all creatures
	+ God becomes and unbecomes, but remains unchanged
	+ God savors Himself in all things, without losing His brightness

• **God's Relationship with Creatures**
	+ Creatures absorb God's light and life, but God remains unchanged
	+ Creatures give up their life to become rational in God
	+ God prepares all creatures for their return to Him

• **The Inner and Outer Man**
	+ The outer man savors creatures as God's gift
	+ The inner man savors creatures as eternity
	+ The inmost man savors creatures as God's gift, but also as eternity

• **The Relationship between God and the Soul**
	+ God is in the soul with His nature, being, and Godhead
	+ The soul is a reflection of God, but remains what it is
	+ God becomes when all creatures say "God"

• **The Godhead**
	+ The Godhead is one, and there is nothing to be said about it
	+ The Godhead does no work, as it is not active
	+ God and Godhead are distinguished by working and not-working

• **The Breakthrough and Outflowing**
	+ When returning to God, the breakthrough will be nobler than the outflowing
	+ The breakthrough is when the soul enters the Godhead
	+ The outflowing is when the soul brings creatures out of their reason into God's reason

• **The Return to the Godhead**
	+ When the soul returns to the Godhead, it will not be asked where it came from
	+ The soul will not be missed, as it will be in its true state
	+ God unbecomes in the sense that the soul is no longer separate from the Godhead

• **The Importance of Poverty and Exile**
	+ Those who follow God in poverty and exile will achieve what others cannot
	+ Those who settle down and serve God will remain in error
	+ The eternal truth declares that poverty and exile are necessary for spiritual growth

• **The Mirror Analogy**
	+ The mirror reflects the sun's light, but does not diminish it
	+ The soul is like the mirror, reflecting God's light and nature
	+ The soul remains what it is, while reflecting God's nature

• **The Conclusion**
	+ Whoever has understood this sermon is blessed
	+ The sermon is a call to follow God in poverty and exile
	+ The eternal truth is declared, and those who follow it will be rewarded.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **The Eternal Wisdom**
	+ The eternal wisdom of the Father can only be heard by those who are within, at home, and one with God.
	+ Three things prevent us from hearing the eternal Word: corporeality, multiplicity, and temporality.
* **Self-Surrender**
	+ To hear the Word of God, one must abandon self and surrender completely.
	+ The hearer is the same as the heard in the eternal Word.
* **God's Nature and Being**
	+ God teaches us that we are the same as His only-begotten Son.
	+ Whatever God performs and teaches, He does so that we may become the only-begotten Son.
* **Love and Identity**
	+ If you love yourself, you love all men as yourself.
	+ To truly love oneself, one must love all men as oneself, including God.
* **Perfect Love**
	+ Perfect love is not loving one person more than another, but loving all men as oneself.
	+ St. Paul's statement about being willing to be separated from God for the sake of his friend and God's sake is an example of perfect love.
* **St. Paul's Statement**
	+ St. Paul's statement means that he would leave everything that he could get from God, including his own salvation, for the sake of God and his friend.
	+ This is an example of leaving God for God, where God is left with the individual in a self-identity that is essential to God's nature.
* **Perfection**
	+ St. Paul's statement is an example of standing in the fullness of perfection.
	+ Perfection is not about being separated from God, but about being one with God in a self-identity that is essential to God's nature.
* **God's Delight and Joy**
	+ God has delight and joy in abundance when He reveals His Godhead and being to us.
	+ This is because God hastens to make it our own, just as it is His own.
* **Standing in God's Ken**
	+ When we stand in God's ken and love, we become none other than what God is Himself.
	+ This is the ultimate goal of human existence.
* **The Abysm of God's Godhead**
	+ The abysm of God's Godhead and the plenitude of His being and nature are revealed to us when we become the only-begotten Son.

	+ A person with good will, who gives up the world for God's sake, will be rewarded with eternal life.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Analogy of Eye and Wood**
	+ The speaker uses an analogy to explain a spiritual concept
	+ The analogy is between the eye and wood, where the eye sees the wood, but the wood remains unchanged
	+ The speaker argues that the eye and wood are "eye-wood" in the act of seeing, and that the wood is not affected by the eye's vision

• **Unity of Being**
	+ The speaker argues that the eye is more united with the eye of a sheep across the sea than it is with its own ear
	+ This is because the eye of the sheep has the same function as the speaker's eye
	+ The speaker imputes more unity of action to the sheep's eye than to its own eyes and ears

• **The Light in the Soul**
	+ The speaker refers to a light in the soul that is uncreated and unchangeable
	+ This light is the light that lays hold of God, unveiled and bare
	+ The speaker argues that this light is far more united with God than it is with any of the powers of the soul

• **Equality of Powers**
	+ The speaker argues that the powers of the soul are all one and equally noble in their being
	+ However, when considered in their functions, one power is much higher and nobler than the others
	+ The speaker uses the example of hearing and sight to illustrate this point

• **Attaining Oneness and Blessedness**
	+ The speaker argues that a person can attain oneness and blessedness in their soul's spark by turning away from self and created things
	+ This spark is opposed to all creatures and wants nothing but God, naked and unveiled
	+ The speaker argues that this light is not satisfied with the unity of the divine nature, but seeks to know its source and ground

• **The Ground of Being**
	+ The speaker refers to the ground of being as an impartible stillness, motionless in itself
	+ This ground is the source of all motion and life in the universe
	+ The speaker argues that the light in the soul finds satisfaction in this ground, and is more one there than it is in itself

• **The Nature of God**
	+ The speaker argues that the light in the soul is not satisfied with the simple, changeless divine being
	+ Instead, it seeks to know the source and ground of this being
	+ The speaker suggests that this ground is beyond all distinction and property, and is the source of all life and motion

• **The Goal of Spiritual Life**
	+ The speaker argues that the goal of spiritual life is to attain oneness and blessedness in the soul's spark
	+ This involves turning away from self and created things, and seeking to know the source and ground of being
	+ The speaker suggests that this goal is attainable through the eternal truth of which he has spoken

• **The Role of Reason**
	+ The speaker argues that reason is necessary for spiritual life
	+ The light in the soul seeks to know the source and ground of being, and reason is the means by which this is achieved
	+ The speaker suggests that the eternal truth of which he has spoken can help us live rationally and attain spiritual life

• **Conclusion**
	+ The speaker concludes by expressing his hope that the eternal truth of which he has spoken will help us attain spiritual life and oneness with God
	+ The speaker ends with a prayer, asking for the help of the eternal truth to live rationally and attain spiritual life.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **Sermon Sixty-One**
	+ Based on Isaiah 11:1-2, "Out of the root of Jesse there shall come forth a rod, and out of the rod shall come forth a flower, and on the flower the spirit of the Lord shall rest and repose."

	+ Blossoms in the ground of the soul
	+ The Holy Ghost sprouts from its root
* **Rest and Repose**
	+ Rest is complete lack of motion and deprivation of its very nature
	+ God's characteristic is immutability
* **The Flower and the Spirit of the Lord**
	+ The flower that blossoms, delight, is the Holy Ghost
	+ The spirit of the Lord shall rest and repose on the flower
* **Prayer**
	+ Let us pray to our dear Lord that we may so rest in Him.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **God's Infinite Love**

	+ The goal of loving God is to come to a place of perfect love, beyond human understanding.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Sermon Sixty-Three**
	+ Based on John 15:4 and Sirach 14:22
	+ Explores the concept of "abiding in God"

• **Three Requirements for Abiding in God**
	+ 1. Renounce self and all things, and not remain attached to anything
		- Must let go of worldly attachments and desires
	+ 2. Love God for Himself, not for any external reason
		- Must love God for His inherent goodness, not for His gifts or rewards
	+ 3. Apprehend God in His pure and naked substance
		- Must strip away external attributes and see God as He truly is

• **Consequences of Abiding in God**
	+ 1. Unity with God
		- No difference or multiplicity between man and God
	+ 2. Shared blessedness with God
		- Man gains blessedness in the same purity as God
	+ 3. Shared knowing, action, and awareness with God
		- Man has one knowing, action, and awareness with God
	+ 4. God is born in the man
		- God's image in man is revealed when man lays bare the divine image

• **Understanding "Birth" in the Context of Abiding in God**
	+ Birth refers to the revelation of God's image in man
	+ This occurs when man discloses the divine image created in him by nature

• **Key Concepts**
	+ "Abide in me" (John 15:4)
	+ "Blessed is the man that dwells in wisdom" (Sirach 14:22)
	+ "God-colored" (Got var)
	+ "Modeless" (Weselos)

• **Scriptural References**
	+ John 15:4
	+ Sirach 14:22
	+ Dionysius the Areopagite, De caelesti hierarchia 7.3
	+ St. Bernard de Clairvaux, De diligendo Deo, 1.1
	+ Isidore, Etymologiae 15.1

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **God's revelation in man**
	+ When God's image is revealed in a man, God is born in him.
	+ The more God's image is revealed, the more God is born in him.

• **Man's growth in likeness to God**
	+ As God's image is revealed in a man, he grows in likeness to God.
	+ The more a man lays himself bare, the more like God he becomes.

• **Man's oneness with God**
	+ Man's oneness with God is a likeness of image, not a created nature.
	+ When we consider man as God, we refer to his Godlike image, not his creaturehood.

• **Christ as God and man**
	+ Christ is both God and man, and when we consider his humanity, we deny his Godhead.
	+ This is not a negation of his creaturehood, but an assertion about God.

• **Love and transformation**
	+ When a man loves God, he is transformed in the divine uniformity.
	+ He becomes one with God and brings forth all creatures with God.

• **Fruits of being one with God**
	+ When a man is one with God, he brings forth all creatures with God.
	+ He bestows bliss on all creatures by virtue of being one with God.

• **Wisdom as a maternal name**
	+ Wisdom is a maternal name that implies passivity.
	+ In God, we posit both activity (the Father) and passivity (the Son).

• **The Son as eternal born Wisdom**
	+ The Son is the eternal born Wisdom, in which all things are distinguished.
	+ Therefore, it is said, "Blessed is the man that dwells in wisdom."

• **The power of the soul**
	+ There are two powers in the soul: one is the man and the other is the woman.
	+ The power of the soul we call "man" is the soul's highest power, wherein God is a naked light.

• **Abiding in the power of the soul**
	+ We ought to abide all the time in the power of the soul, which is the highest power.
	+ In this power, all things are the same, and we take them as being in God.

	+ The soul wells up in the Holy Ghost, where there is one life, one being, and one work.
	+ The Father's work is one, and He makes the soul His only-begotten Son with no distinction.

* **Love and Detachment**
	+ Loving God with all our heart means loving our neighbor as ourselves
	+ This leads to an equal love for all things and possession of them
* **The Nature of Love**
	+ Love begins with God and extends to our neighbor
	+ When we love God equally, we love all things equally
* **The Limitations of Material Things**
	+ Material things differ from spiritual things in that they can be possessed and enjoyed
	+ However, true possession and enjoyment come from detachment and love
* **The Relationship between God and Humanity**
	+ When we become one with God, we are blessed with the blessings of all humanity
	+ This means we are blessed by the virtues and honor of others
* **The Importance of Detachment**
	+ Detachment is necessary to possess and enjoy spiritual things
	+ It is also necessary to love God and our neighbor equally
* **The Result of Detachment**
	+ When we are detached from worldly things, we possess them and receive spiritual blessings
	+ This leads to a life of love, detachment, and spiritual possession.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text of Sermon 74 by Meister Eckhart:

• **Spiritual things are not separate**
	+ Unlike physical things, spiritual things are not separate from one another.
	+ What is in one person is fully contained in another.

• **The highest and lowest are equal**
	+ The highest angel has nothing that is not in the lowest angel.
	+ Neither being nor blessedness is exclusive to one person.

• **Abandoning worldly things**
	+ When people consider themselves or their own possessions, they have not truly abandoned anything.
	+ True abandonment is necessary for spiritual growth.

• **The value of commonality**
	+ The more a thing is in common, the nobler and more valuable it is.
	+ Life and being are the most valuable things, as they are shared by all living creatures.

• **The importance of being**
	+ Being is the most intimate and dearest thing to all creatures.
	+ It flows directly from God and is the foundation of life.

• **The universality of life**
	+ The more universal our life is, the better and nobler it is.
	+ True poverty and detachment are necessary for spiritual growth.

• **The example of St. Francis**
	+ St. Francis abandoned all worldly things and loved his neighbor as himself.
	+ This is an example of true poverty and detachment.

• **The connection to St. Peter**
	+ St. Peter's statement "We have abandoned all things" is not truly possible if one considers what they will gain.
	+ True abandonment requires a willingness to let go of all worldly attachments.

• **The relationship between being and life**
	+ Being flows directly from God and is the foundation of life.
	+ Life flows from being and is more valuable than mere existence.

• **The goal of spiritual growth**
	+ The goal of spiritual growth is to become pleasing to God and to give up all worldly attachments.
	+ This requires true poverty, detachment, and a willingness to love one's neighbor as oneself.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Lord's Ascension**
	+ The Lord's disciples were troubled by his announcement of his ascension into heaven
	+ The Lord consoled them, knowing that love drives out fear (1 John 4:18)

• **The Role of Fear**
	+ Fear is painful and can be a barrier to love
	+ However, fear can be useful in the beginning of the good life, leading us to love
	+ Love binds us to God, while fear passes out

• **The Soul's Nature**
	+ The soul is created by God and belongs to heaven by nature
	+ The soul is a light, pure and unadulterated, created without distinction from God

• **Descriptions of the Soul**
	+ The soul is a spirit, made in God's image
	+ The soul is a fire, powerful and lofty, that can never rest except in God
	+ The soul is a spark of divine and celestial nature

• **The Soul's Relationship with God**
	+ The soul is created to return to God
	+ The soul can never rest but in God
	+ The soul is drawn to God like a fire is drawn to the heavens

• **The Soul's Condition**
	+ Some souls are dense and follow haltingly, like air lagging behind fire
	+ Some souls are gross and earthbound, like water that cannot keep pace with fire
	+ Some souls are moved by desire, but do not rise to the heavens

• **The Lord's Ascension as a Benefit**
	+ The Lord's ascension shows that the soul is created for heaven
	+ The soul belongs entirely to heaven, and the Lord's ascension proves this

• **The Body and the Soul**
	+ The body is composed of the four elements (earth, air, water, fire)
	+ The soul is a spark of divine and celestial nature, separate from the body

• **The Lord's Soul**
	+ The Lord's soul is a spark sent to heaven, proving that the common resting place of all souls is in heaven

• **The Implication of the Lord's Ascension**
	+ The Lord's ascension shows that the soul is created for heaven and belongs entirely to heaven.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Soul's Relationship with the Body**
	+ The soul is intimately united with the body
	+ They must stay together eternally, despite the body belonging to earth and the soul to heaven

• **God's Solution**
	+ God became man to solve the problem of the soul's relationship with the body
	+ By becoming man, God sent a part of earth to heaven, making the entire earth belong to heaven

• **The Ascension's Benefits**
	+ Our Lord Jesus Christ's ascension shows us how to prepare ourselves to follow him
	+ He said "I go to prepare a place for you" (John 14:2)

• **Preparing the Soul**
	+ The soul must be lifted up or made ready by four things:
		- Being rescued from instability and the tempests of the world
		- Herding up animal passions and desires into the highest power of the soul
		- Being raised above time and place to receive divine works
		- Freeing the soul's desire from mortal and perishable things

• **The Importance of Knowledge and Love**
	+ Knowledge and love allow us to transcend the world
	+ By knowledge and love, we are not in the world (St. Augustine)

• **Seeing and Knowing God's Works**
	+ We can see and know God's works, but not perfectly in this life
	+ Moses saw the burning bush but could not go right up to it

• **The Significance of Taking Off Shoes**
	+ Taking off shoes signifies freeing the soul's desire from mortal and perishable things
	+ It means withdrawing from all things that can be lost or destroyed

• **The Example of Moses**
	+ Moses' experience with the burning bush teaches us four precious lessons
	+ Moses was rescued from instability, herded up his animal passions, raised above time, and freed his desire from mortal things

• **The Importance of Raising the Soul**
	+ The soul must be raised above time and place to receive divine works
	+ This is necessary for the Holy Ghost to enter and operate in the soul

• **Conclusion**
	+ May God help us to prepare ourselves to follow our Lord Jesus Christ
	+ May we be lifted up and made ready to receive divine works and union with God.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Earth is Full of Mercy**
	+ King David says the earth is full of the mercy of the Lord (Psalm 32:5)
	+ St. Augustine comments that the earth is full of pity due to its pain and lamentation

• **The Heavens are Established by God's Power**
	+ King David says the heavens are established by the power of the word of the Lord (Psalm 32:6)
	+ St. Augustine interprets this as the power of the Father, wisdom of the Son, and goodness of the Holy Ghost

• **The Holy Trinity is Revealed in the Psalm**
	+ The Psalm reveals the Holy Trinity through the power of the Father, wisdom of the Son, and goodness of the Holy Ghost

• **St. Paul's Experience in the Third Heaven**
	+ St. Paul was caught up into the third heaven and saw things that are ineffable
	+ He cried out with a loud voice, "O thou depth of the riches and wisdom and knowledge of God"

• **Three Kinds of Knowledge**
	+ St. Augustine interprets St. Paul's experience as three kinds of knowledge:
		- Knowledge of creatures (perceived through the five senses)
		- Spiritual knowledge (perceived through likeness and absence)
		- Purely spiritual knowledge (perceived without sound, matter, or appearance)

• **The Third Heaven is Purely Spiritual Knowledge**
	+ In the third heaven, the soul is rapt away from all objective, bodily things
	+ The soul knows God as being onefold in nature and threefold in the Persons

• **St. John's Experience of Gnosis**
	+ St. John says that the light enlightens all who come into this world
	+ He means that he was aware of nothing but God and all things divine

• **The Pure Perception of the Holy Trinity**
	+ St. John's experience is a pure perception of the Holy Trinity
	+ He says that God is "the Word in the beginning, and the Word is with God, and the Word is God"

• **The Heavens are Established by God's Word**
	+ St. Paul says that the heavens are established by the word of the Lord
	+ Job also says that the heavens are established as if they were cast in bronze (Job 37:18)

• **Four Things to Note about Heaven**
	+ Heaven is fixed and established by God's power
	+ Heaven is a place of pure perception and knowledge of God
	+ Heaven is a place of spiritual knowledge and awareness of the Holy Trinity
	+ Heaven is a place of illumination and enlightenment

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **Heaven is a model for human nature**
	+ Heaven is pure, fruitful, and contains all things
	+ Humans should strive to be like heaven, a dwelling place for God
* **Stability and consistency**
	+ A good person remains unchanged, like the heavens
	+ Their will is one with God's will, unaffected by good or evil
* **Purity and clarity**
	+ Heaven is pure and clear, like still water
	+ Humans are defiled by earthly things, but can strive for purity
* **Adaptation and receptivity**
	+ The soul is whole in every member, but not all parts are equally pure
	+ The hand and foot cannot receive spiritual things like the eye can
* **Spiritual awareness**
	+ A carnal person cannot know spiritual things like a spiritual person
	+ Seeking to know spiritual things without being prepared can lead to misguidance
* **Sleep and spiritual state**
	+ A good person dreams good things in their sleep, a sign of purity
	+ A bad person may also dream good things, but a good person fights evil in their sleep
* **Love and unity**
	+ Heaven comprehends all things and retains them
	+ A person can attain this in love, containing all things in themselves
* **Fruitfulness and productivity**
	+ Heaven is fruitful, helping all kinds of work
	+ Heaven is the throne of our Lord, where all things are one being
* **Unity and oneness**
	+ A heathen master says that if there were no time, place, or matter, all would be one being
	+ The soul longs to be pressed into God like wax into a seal
* **Conclusion and prayer**
	+ May the good God help us to attain this state of unity and purity
	+ Amen.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **God is Love**
	+ The sermon is based on 1 John 4:16, "God is love, and he who dwells in love dwells in God, and God in him."
	+ The preacher says "God is Love" to emphasize the unity of God

• **Reasons for saying "God is Love"**
	+ There are four reasons for saying "God is Love"
	+ The preacher will explain each reason in the following points

• **First reason: God pursues all creatures with His love**
	+ God's love is a good that pursues all creatures
	+ Creatures are drawn to God because of His love
	+ God takes joy in being pursued by creatures

• **Second reason: All creatures pursue God with their love**
	+ Even evil actions are motivated by a desire for love and peace
	+ Creatures are drawn to God because they desire love
	+ Even inanimate objects, like trees, would pursue God with love if they had reason

• **Third reason: God has scattered His love among all creatures**
	+ God's love is present in all creatures
	+ Every creature has something lovable about it
	+ Creatures love something in God, even if they don't know it

• **Fourth reason: The soul can never be satisfied but with love**
	+ The soul is drawn to God because of His love
	+ Love is the only thing that can satisfy the soul
	+ St. Augustine says that even if God gave us everything, we would not be satisfied unless we had God Himself

• **The unity of God**
	+ The preacher emphasizes the unity of God
	+ Saying "God is Love" emphasizes the unity of God's nature

• **The importance of love**
	+ Love is the only thing that can satisfy the soul
	+ Creatures are drawn to God because of His love
	+ Love is the foundation of all relationships with God

• **The role of reason in loving God**
	+ Even inanimate objects would pursue God with love if they had reason
	+ Creatures are drawn to God because they desire love
	+ Reason helps us to understand and appreciate God's love

• **The preacher's use of biblical and non-biblical sources**
	+ The preacher quotes St. Augustine and other sources to support his arguments
	+ The sermon is based on biblical and non-biblical sources, including Aristotle and the Song of Solomon

• **The theme of the sermon**
	+ The theme of the sermon is the unity of God and the importance of love
	+ The preacher emphasizes the idea that God is love and that love is the foundation of all relationships with God.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **The Nature of Desire**
	+ People often desire things because of their appearance, not the thing itself
	+ This leads to a cycle of desire and dissatisfaction
	+ The true object of desire is God, who is beyond human comprehension

	+ We often love things because of what they represent, not the thing itself
	+ This is like loving a picture on a wall because of the wall, not the picture
	+ To truly love something, we must love it for itself, not what it represents
* **The Nobility of Angels**
	+ Angels are numerous and have a high level of nobility
	+ If an angel's nobility were to fall to earth, it would bring about great fruitfulness
	+ The highest angel is of even greater nobility

	+ God is not found in creation, but only in Himself
	+ Creation is like a mere splinter compared to God's greatness
* **God as Love**
	+ God must love all creatures with His love, whether they know it or not
	+ We should not pray to God for His gifts, but rather for the ability to receive them
	+ God's love is the source of all blessings
* **The Nature of the Soul**
	+ The soul that dwells in love is everywhere, just like God
	+ God is in the soul, and the soul is in God
	+ This creates a unity between God and the soul, with no separation

	+ The soul that dwells in love is also one All, without anything outside of God
	+ This unity is the essence of the sermon for All Saints
* **The Importance of Loving God**
	+ To truly love something, we must love it for itself, not what it represents
	+ Loving God is the key to true happiness and fulfillment
	+ We should strive to love God above all else
* **The Peril of Not Loving God**
	+ Not loving God leads to sin and separation from Him
	+ We must be careful to love God above all else
	+ God preserve us from the peril of not loving Him!

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Sermon Seventy-Eight**
	+ The sermon is closely related to Sermon 5
	+ Eckhart gives his own interpretation of the text "Deus caritas est"

• **Interpretation of Deus caritas est**
	+ Eckhart omits the definite article to denote the unitary love of God
	+ He capitalizes "Love" to distinguish it from other loves

• **Quotations from Augustine**
	+ Eckhart quotes Augustine freely, citing Contra Julianum 13.8
	+ He also quotes De Genesi ad litteram 12.26

• **Understanding of Love**
	+ Eckhart explains that when we say "goodness", we mean things, not God
	+ He takes "minnen" as an error for "meinen", meaning "mean" or "intend"

• **Angels and Temporality**
	+ The manuscript has "ain coli" or "an coly", which makes no sense
	+ Quint suggests that it may be corrupted from "caelum" (heaven)

• **God's Relationship to the Soul**
	+ The soul is one with God, but not united
	+ Eckhart uses a simile to explain the difference between unity and oneness

• **Scripture and the Masters**
	+ Scripture says that Moses saw God face to face
	+ The masters deny this, saying that God is one and not two

• **Texts from Scripture**
	+ Eckhart quotes 1 John 4:16 and Matthew 25:21
	+ He also quotes John 17:21 and John 12:26

• **Understanding of Unity**
	+ Eckhart explains that God is "everything" but not "all things"
	+ He means that God is the source of all unity and oneness

• **Conclusion**
	+ Eckhart concludes by emphasizing the soul's oneness with God
	+ He encourages his listeners to note the truth of this oneness

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **The Importance of Unity**
	+ Eckhart emphasizes the importance of unity and warns against supposing that individual forms are better than unity.
	+ He believes that unity is essential for spiritual growth and that individual forms can lead to heresy.
* **The Nature of Unity**
	+ In unity, individual identities are forgotten and merged with the divine.
	+ This unity is not just a state of being, but a way of living and relating to others.
* **The Role of the Intellect**
	+ The intellect is the highest part of the soul and is where the soul dwells in community with angels.
	+ The intellect is free from time and is the seat of spiritual life.
* **The Widow as a Symbol**
	+ The widow in the Gospel story represents the soul that has lost its spiritual life.
	+ The widow's son represents the intellect, which is the man in the soul.
* **The Importance of Living in the Intellect**
	+ Living in the intellect is essential for spiritual growth and for receiving the "living water" of the Holy Ghost.
	+ Those who dwell in the intellect are granted spiritual life and are fruitful in their spiritual birth.
* **The Nature of Spiritual Fruitfulness**
	+ Spiritual fruitfulness is not just a one-time event, but an ongoing process.
	+ The soul that has God is fruitful all the time and gives birth to new spiritual life every moment.
* **God's Work in the World**
	+ God is always at work in the eternal now, bringing forth His Son and creating new life.
	+ God's pleasure in this birth is so great that He spends His whole energy on it.
* **The Nature of Knowledge**
	+ The more one knows all things, the more perfect that knowledge becomes.
	+ However, this knowledge seems like nothing compared to the birth of God's Son.
* **The Importance of Leaving and Forsaking Creatures**
	+ The widow's lack was that her fruitfulness was dead, and accordingly the fruit perished.
	+ We must leave and forsake all creatures in order to live in unity and spiritual life.
* **The Paradox of Spiritual Life**
	+ Spiritual life is not just a state of being, but a way of living and relating to others.
	+ It involves forgetting individual identities and merging with the divine.

	+ The place where God gives birth to His only-begotten Son is a secret and noble place.
	+ This place is above the intellect and will, and is the true union between God and the soul.

• **The Importance of Understanding**
	+ Foolish folk take bad for good and good for bad.
	+ Only those who understand aright the unique work of God in the soul can see its nobility and beauty.

	+ The soul has no reason or will when separated from the body
	+ The soul is purified of a life that is divided and enters a life that is unified

	+ Whatever falls away from this light falls into mortality and dies
	+ The soul should not turn to any object, or it will die

	+ The soul's goal is to be established in pure being
	+ The soul should be helped to this goal by God
	+ May God help us to a life that is united, Amen.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **Sermon Eighty-Seven: Beatitudes**
	+ The sermon is based on Matthew 5:3, "Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven."
	+ The sermon explores the meaning of poverty in the context of the Beatitudes.
* **Two Kinds of Poverty**
	+ External poverty is good and commendable, but not the kind being discussed in this sermon.
	+ Interior poverty is the kind being discussed, which is a state of being free from attachment and desire.
* **Understanding the Sermon**
	+ The preacher asks the audience to be like the truth being spoken about in order to understand the sermon.
	+ The preacher warns that unless the audience is like this truth, they will not be able to follow the sermon.
* **Defining a Poor Man**
	+ A poor man is one who wants nothing, knows nothing, and has nothing.
	+ This definition is being explored in a higher sense, beyond the external appearance of poverty.
* **Wanting Nothing**
	+ Some people misunderstand the concept of wanting nothing, thinking it means doing God's will.
	+ However, this is not true poverty, as it still implies a will to serve God's will.
	+ True poverty means being free from one's created will and desiring nothing.
* **The Nature of True Poverty**
	+ A poor man is one who wills nothing and desires nothing.
	+ This state of being is characterized by a complete lack of attachment and desire.
* **The Preacher's Experience**
	+ The preacher reflects on their own experience of being free from attachment and desire in their pre-existent state.
	+ In this state, they were their own cause and had no desire for anything else.
* **The Importance of True Poverty**
	+ The preacher emphasizes that true poverty is a state of being that is essential for understanding the sermon.
	+ Those who do not understand true poverty are not able to follow the sermon.
* **The Preacher's Warning**
	+ The preacher warns that those who cling to external practices and appearances of poverty are not truly poor.
	+ These individuals are ignorant of the actual nature of divine truth and are not truly following God's will.
* **The Goal of the Sermon**
	+ The preacher's goal is to speak about a truth that few good people can understand.
	+ The sermon is an exploration of the nature of true poverty and its relationship to the Beatitudes.

	+ We should not seek reward or recognition for our giving, but only that God may be glorified

	+ Those who hear and keep God's word are blessed
	+ This blessing is greater than any honor or glory given to Mary, the mother of Christ

• **The Honor Due to Those Who Hear and Keep God's Word**
	+ Those who hear and keep God's word should be given greater honor and glory than Mary
	+ This honor is due to them because they are blessed and have a special relationship with God

• **The Father's Relationship with His Word**
	+ The Father hears, knows, and speaks only His Word
	+ The Father's Word is His nature, and He begets it unceasingly

• **The Nature of the Father's Word**
	+ The Father's Word is not spoken by His will, but naturally and eternally
	+ The Father's Word is rooted in His nature, and He must speak it unceasingly

• **The Father's Speaking of Our Spirits**
	+ The Father speaks our spirits in the same Word that He speaks His own nature
	+ This speaking makes us the natural sons of God, equal to the Word

• **The Significance of Being a Natural Son of God**
	+ Being a natural son of God is a great blessing and privilege
	+ It is a relationship that is equal to the Word, and is rooted in the Father's nature.

• **The Father's Knowledge**

	+ The Father's knowledge is identical to the Word
	+ The Father's knowledge is not separate from the Word

• **The Manner of Fruitfulness**
	+ Jesus' noble soul is a grain of wheat that perished in the earth of his humanity
	+ This grain of wheat was grieved unto death in suffering and action
	+ The fruit of this grain of wheat is the union of human and divine nature in one Christ

• **The Union of Human and Divine**
	+ Jesus knew himself as uniting human and divine nature in one moment of time
	+ This union is the fruit of the grain of wheat
	+ This union is the true God and true man, one Christ who is God

	+ Some masters say the eternal birth is wrought by the Father's personal power.
	+ However, the eternal Word originates in the essential mind, so it must be performed by the Father's natural power.

	+ The Father-nature has a maternal name when it combines with its unity of nature.
	+ In this case, it is doing mother's work, which is to conceive.

• **The essential nature of the eternal birth**
	+ The eternal birth is essential to the Father's nature.
	+ If the Father were to stop for an instant, He would deny Himself.

	+ However, they have a difference in their Persons.

• **The office of the Son**
	+ The Son does not have the office of begetting another Son.
	+ This is because it is not his task, and if he did, he would be the Person of the Father.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Identity of Essence and Being**
	+ The identity of essence and being is only found in God.
	+ This is a fundamental concept in Eckhart's theology.

• **Three Powers of the Soul**
	+ The three powers of the soul are concupiscibilis (desire), irascibilis (anger), and rationalis (reason).
	+ These powers are discussed in various sermons, including Sermon 50, 52, and 97.

• **General Perfection of Divine Goodness**
	+ The source of the soul's perfection is the "general perfection" of divine goodness.
	+ This perfection is one with the divine ground of being.

• **Augustine's Influence**
	+ Eckhart's ideas are influenced by Augustine's Contra Julianum.
	+ Eckhart's own ideas are based on Augustine's work, but also contain original insights.

• **Putting on Christ**
	+ St. Paul's phrase "Put on Christ" (Romans 13:14) is central to Eckhart's sermon.
	+ Putting off self allows the soul to take in Christ, God, blessedness, and holiness.

	+ The soul is created for a great estate, which is to be like God.
	+ The soul is made in the image of God, but also like God's nature, essence, and activity.

	+ This reception is without difference or distance.

• **The Nameless Thing in the Soul**
	+ There is a nameless thing in the soul where God is bare.
	+ This thing has no being of its own, but is what it is in another.

• **Entering into God**
	+ The soul can enter into God, into blessedness.
	+ This entry is possible through the reception of God's influence.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Sermon 93: "For sometimes you were darkness, but now a light in God" (Ephesians 5:8)**
	+ St. Paul says that we were once in darkness, but now we are a light in God.
	+ The prophets who walked in the light knew and discovered the secret truth by the influence of the Holy Ghost.

• **Reasons for the prophets' silence**
	+ They were afraid to lie by trying to conceptualize the vast and arcane goodness of God.
	+ They couldn't find an image or form to express the sublime truth they saw in God.
	+ They saw the hidden truth, but couldn't find words to express it.

• **Interpretation of "Aliquando"**
	+ "Aliquando" means "sometimes" and implies time, which keeps us from the light.
	+ Time is opposed to God, and clinging to time is a barrier to knowing God.

• **The soul and time**
	+ The soul, created above the heavens, has no truck with time in its highest and purest part.
	+ The soul is outpoured and reformed into its primal and imageless image through the procession of the three Persons.

• **The image of the Godhead in the soul**
	+ The image of the Godhead is imprinted on the soul through the outflowing of the Holy Ghost.
	+ This image is permanent and lasting, not like a picture that fades.

• **Knowing things in their cause**
	+ To know things, we must know them in their cause, not just in their effects.
	+ This is what Paul means by "now a light in God".

• **The eternal birth of the Son**
	+ The Father gives birth to the Son in an ever-present Now.
	+ In this eternal birth, the soul flows forth into its essence and the image of the Godhead is imprinted.

• **Debate about the image of God in the soul**
	+ Some masters said that God imprinted His image in the soul like a picture on a wall, which fades.
	+ This was refuted, and other masters said that God impressed His image as something permanent in the soul.

• **The importance of knowing God**
	+ Knowing God is essential for our blessedness.
	+ The prophets and St. Paul teach us to know God through their writings and the influence of the Holy Ghost.

• **The role of the Holy Ghost**
	+ The Holy Ghost influences the prophets and teaches us to know God.
	+ The Holy Ghost proceeds from both the Father and the Son, and is involved in the procession of the three Persons.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **God's Nature**
	+ God is unknowable in His nature
	+ God is without activity in His hidden stillness
	+ God has no name, as creatures are not capable of understanding Him as He is in Himself

• **God's Riches**
	+ God is rich in Himself and in all things
	+ God's riches are fivefold:
		- He is the first cause, pouring Himself forth into all things
		- He is one in essence, the inwardness of all things
		- He is the fountainhead, sharing Himself with all things
		- He is unchangeable, the most dependable
		- He is perfect, the most desirable

• **God's Simplicity**
	+ God is simple in His essence, being intrinsically one without a second
	+ All simple things are maintained by the fact that God is
	+ Creatures are one in the one, and are God in God

• **God's Overflowing**
	+ God flows forth into all things in three ways:
		- With being
		- With life
		- With light
	+ God especially flows forth into the rational soul as a comprehension of all beings and a return of creatures to their original source

• **God's Unchangeableness**
	+ God unites with things while remaining one in Himself
	+ God's unchangeableness and immensity make Him the most dependable
	+ All things are to God as a drop in the ocean, and when the soul imbibes God, it becomes divine but God does not become the soul

• **God's Perfection**
	+ God is the perfection of Himself and all things
	+ God is His own good and the good of all things
	+ All things desire God, for He is their good

• **The Soul's Union with God**
	+ When the soul imbibes God, it becomes divine but God does not become the soul
	+ The soul loses its name and power, but not its will and existence
	+ The will of a man dies, but he abides in God eternally

• **The Goodness of God**
	+ God's goodness is His own good and the good of all things
	+ All things desire God, for He is their good
	+ May that goodness be ours, that we may enjoy it eternally.
